### 2026-05-15 — theorist-critic (Round 2)
**Phase:** Theory pipeline (Strategy/Execution)
**Target:** Round-2 surgical patch of prp:lepskii
**Score:** 39 / 100 (REGRESSION from 62)
**Verdict:** Two CRITICAL errors exposed by displaying previously hand-waved steps: (1) G3 Jensen step in wrong direction — $(E\|\hat e_j\|^8)^{1/4} \le C_8 E\|\hat e_j\|^2$ does not follow from `ass:iterbd`; standard Jensen runs the other way. Hypercontractivity or sub-Gaussian iterates required but not stated. (2) G2 rate algebra reads $\delta_n^2 M_n \to 0$, but `ass:restrvarth`(i) + `ass:mn-growth` give $\delta_n^2 M_n = O(1)$. Required $\delta_n^2 m^2 = o(1)$ fails at $m \asymp M_n$ by factor $\delta_n^{-2}$. Plus G2 Lipschitz step is a non-sequitur from `prp:koper`(a) (quadratic remainder, not derivative Lipschitz); G1 $III_m$ off by $\sqrt m$. **ESCALATE to user.**
**Report:** `quality_reports/theory/volatility-loc-scale/proofs_review_lepskii_round2.md`
**Strike count:** 2 (escalation triggered — see ESCALATION_TARGET in permissions.md)
**Escalation options:** (A) strengthen `ass:rateconv` for hypercontractive bounds on iterates; (B) tighten `ass:mn-growth` to $M_n = o(\delta_n^{-2/3})$; (C) reformulate G2 via Hilbert-scale interpolation.

### 2026-07-28 09:05 — orchestrator (plan amendment 2, user-directed)
**Phase:** Strategy (contract revision, ahead of Stage 2)
**Target:** `quality_reports/plans/2026-07-28_panel-npiv-direct-theta.md` §1, §2.1–§2.6, §2.7, §14; new §17
**Score:** N/A
**Verdict:** **Assumption A1 changed from unconditional to conditional independence at the user's direction.** The plan's original justification for the unconditional form (Remark R1, reasoning from Lemma L1) was a non-sequitur and has been withdrawn: it conflated *identification* of $\theta_\dagger$ — which comes from the mean restriction (B) — with *debiasing*, which comes from (A) and is mean-zero at the truth by construction. "Carries no information about $\theta$" is a property of every Riesz representer, not a disqualification. L1 itself is retained and correct; only the inference drawn from it was wrong.
**Decision:** A1 is $\Delta U \perp W \mid \Delta Z$ with $E[\Delta U\mid\Delta Z]=0$; the **full** conditional restriction is exploited, with the curse of dimensionality in $\Delta Z$ handled by **Bierens' device** (user's proposal) — the conditional moment restriction becomes a continuum of unconditional moments indexed by $t$, so no conditional distribution is estimated given $\Delta Z$.
**Consequences recorded in the plan:** third nuisance $\varrho(\Delta Z)=E[W\mid\Delta Z]$; new Assumption A4 (overlap — non-trivial at a 689/300 split); new target result `lem:bierens`; `prp:orthog` gains parts (ii) and (iii); normalisation N1 ($E[L\mid\Delta Z]=0$) becomes load-bearing. Remainder is now a sum of three pairwise nuisance products, and $\hat\varphi$ inherits $\hat\varrho$'s error through $\widetilde W$ in the LF step — the proof must not omit that induced term.
**Verified before committing to the contract:** N1 is free and compatible with P1(i) — shifting $L$ by any $c(\Delta Z)$ leaves $E[\widetilde W(L+c)]=0$ and leaves $\ell=\partial_u L$ unchanged, so the adjoint equation is untouched while $\partial_\varrho E[\psi]=-E[r\,E[L\mid\Delta Z]]$ vanishes.
**Net theoretical gain:** A2 (the binding source condition) is *weaker* under the conditional route — the direction family grows from $\{\lambda(u)\}$ to $\{\lambda(u)w(t'\Phi(z))\}$, strictly enlarging $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{A}^\ast_{\mathrm{cond}})$. Cost is over-identification of $\theta$, which is lost.
**Flag:** `scripts/R/orthogonal_theta.R` now implements a superseded estimator (unconditional `wbar`, kernel over scalar $u$, unconditional centring). Change is contained (§17.5) but must happen before Stage 4.

**Three findings that change the paper, not just the bibliography:**
1. **`poirier2017` is a genuine near-miss already sitting unexploited in the project bib** — efficiency bound and $\sqrt n$ efficient estimation for a finite-dimensional parameter under conditional *and* unconditional independence, via "a growing number of zero-covariance conditions asymptotically equivalent to the independence restriction" (a cousin of the Bierens family), illustrated on IV and partially linear models. Should be the primary citation for `prp:effbound`. Argued to fall short because his nuisances are well-posed and independence there strengthens mean-independence rather than being the sole source of information about $\varphi$.
2. **Chen & Pouzo (2012, 2015) is a framework-level overlap the paper must answer explicitly** — under the amended A1 the model *is* a conditional moment model with nonsmooth generalized residuals. Four-part rebuttal drafted (continuum of residuals vs fixed $d_\rho$; no smoothing in $\Delta Z$; LF iterate with data-driven stopping is not Donsker; no negative result, no panel).
3. **Concurrency risk is elevated and dated** — Escanciano, *Locally Robust Kernel Specification Tests for Conditional Moment Restrictions* (arXiv:2607.24382), posted **one day before the search**, combines Neyman-orthogonal moments + cross-fitting + RKHS. Different target (specification testing, no ill-posed nuisance, no NPIV), so not a scoop, but the window is estimated at 12–24 months rather than 3 years.
**Independent convergence worth recording:** the librarian concludes that the Bierens weight-family condition and the characteristic-kernel condition are the *same requirement*, that Fan & Li (2000) get halfway, and that the full statement appears absent from econometrics — reached independently of, and agreeing with, plan §17.5, which was written before this report landed. Candidate free secondary contribution.
**Plan §5 to-dos closed:** `florens2006` confirmed as the published EctJ 15(2):304–324, 2012 version (key name misleads only); Stock & Wright (2000) added.
**Open gaps:** Poirier full text unobtainable (SSRN 403, preprint 404) — carried as a gate item; 8 entries marked `% UNVERIFIED`; Semantic Scholar and arXiv API rate-limited throughout, forward chains run on OpenAlex instead.

### 2026-07-28 12:10 — **GATE ITEM G-1 CLOSED. NOVELTY VERDICT: CLEAR (UNCONDITIONAL).**
**Phase:** Discovery — gate closure
**Target:** Poirier (2017), *Efficient estimation in models with independence restrictions*
**Source:** the user located the paper locally at `~/Downloads/Poirier(2015).pdf` — the working-paper version. PDF metadata title matches the published title exactly; 22 pages, matching JoE 196(1), 1–22. Text extracted with `pdftotext -layout` and read directly. **First-party evidence at last; no inference required.**

**Answer to the narrow question — does any example put an ENDOGENOUS regressor inside the NONPARAMETRIC component? NO.**

- **Example 1.2 (Instrumental Variables Regression):** $Y = X'\theta_0 + U$ with $U \perp Z$. Poirier's own words: *"This is a **linear IV model** with independent errors."* Parametric throughout — there is no nonparametric component at all. This resolves the ambiguity the critic flagged: "instrumental variables regression model" meant **linear IV**, not NPIV.
- **Example 1.5 (Partially Linear Regression):** $Y = X\theta_0 + G_0(W,\varepsilon)$ with $\varepsilon \perp (X,W)$. The nonparametric component's argument $W$ is **exogenous** by assumption. Described as *"a generalization of the Robinson (1988) partially linear regression model which allows the marginal effect of $W$ on $Y$ to vary across observationally equivalent units"*, following **Santos (2011)**. Reduces to $\rho(Y,X,\theta) \perp X \mid W$.
- **Monte Carlo (§4):** Card (1999) returns-to-schooling design, binary instrument, endogenous $X$ = years of schooling, $Y = X\theta_0 + U$ — again fully **linear**.

**The single "ill-posed inverse problem" mention is not about a structural nuisance.** In context (§1.2) it concerns the **CGMM optimal weighting operator**: *"This CGMM (Continuum of GMM) estimator will be efficient when the optimal weighting operator is used… More generally, this problem suffers from the ill-posed inverse problem, and a Tikhonov regularization is suggested. **By comparison, the estimator we propose uses a finite but growing number of zero-covariance conditions**…"* Poirier raises ill-posedness only to explain how he **avoids** it. Round 1's confabulated "the IV example is an ill-posed inverse problem requiring regularization" was wrong in substance, though the words do appear in the paper for an unrelated reason.

**Stronger than a clearance — the nearest competitor names this paper's contribution as his own open problem.** Poirier's §5 conclusion, verbatim: *"Several extensions of this paper may be interesting. The first would be to generalize our results to models with unconditional independence restriction **with nuisance functions of the type $\rho(Y,X,\theta,h(\cdot)) \perp Z$, where $h(\cdot)$ is a nonparametric object**."* That is precisely this paper's setting, and he explicitly does not do it. This is the single best positioning sentence available and it should go in the introduction.

**Two by-products worth carrying into Stage 2:**
1. **Precedent for plan §14's open $\mu$ question.** Poirier faced the same design choice — a continuum of moments whose optimal weighting operator is ill-posed — and resolved it with a *finite but growing* basis ($K$, $L$) rather than Tikhonov on the continuum. His conclusion also flags choosing $K$ and $L$ by higher-order MSE expansions, citing **Donald et al. (2008)**, as unfinished. This connects directly to the CGMM block (critic finding F-2) and gives a concrete, citable alternative to inventing a weighting rule.
2. **Precedent for the A1 discussion.** Poirier has a section titled *"Conditional independence restrictions and partially linear regression example"* showing that *"imposing a conditional moment restriction instead of conditional independence restriction results in a loss of identification."* That is adjacent to this paper's Lemma L1 / Remark R1 territory and should be cited there — note the direction differs (in this paper the conditional-independence restriction is uninformative about $\theta$ and identification comes from the mean restriction).

**Verdict:** **CLEAR, unconditional.** No escalation. Plan §5's novelty-verification instruction is discharged on first-party evidence.

### 2026-07-28 12:45 — strategist (proof-strategy memo, round 1 COMPLETE)
**Phase:** Strategy
**Target:** `quality_reports/strategy/panel-npiv-discrete/proof_strategy_memo.md` (937 lines, all nine targets, six required headings + §7 simulation grid (14 rows) and §8 counterexample probes (9 rows))
**Score:** pending strategist-critic (gate ≥ 80)
**Verdict:** Substantive. It found a real error in the plan's §2.6 — one the orchestrator had introduced — and raised two further findings that change the architecture rather than the exposition.

**Finding 1 — the $n^{-1/4}$ wall. Verified independently before acceptance; plan §2.6 corrected.** §2.6 claimed the remainder was three pairwise products over $(\varphi, L, \varrho)$. A second-order expansion has six terms: two vanish exactly (the moment is affine in $L$ and in $\varrho$ separately), and four survive — **including $\|\hat\varphi-\vdagger\|^2$, a square rather than a product.** It arises because $L$ is nonlinear in the residual: expanding $L(\Delta U_\dagger - \Delta h)$ and cancelling first-order terms via P1(i) leaves $\tfrac12E[\widetilde W\,\partial_u\ell_\dagger(\Delta h)^2]$. A product of two errors is $o_P(n^{-1/2})$ when each is $o_P(n^{-1/4})$; a square gets no such help. **This forces $\|\hat\varphi-\vdagger\| = o_P(n^{-1/4})$ outright**, attainable under mild ill-posedness when $s > a+\tfrac12$ and — per the memo — unattainable at any smoothness under severe ill-posedness. `assapp4`'s $\kappa_n\to0$ device makes it worse by $\kappa_n^{-1}$. **It binds even if A2 and the tangential cone condition both hold, so it dominates the §2.6b question.** A2 promoted to **A2′**.
**Finding 2 — the RKHS kernel makes the §15 bound vacuous.** The balancing bound is $\lVert\mu_g\rVert_{\mathcal H}\lVert\hat\varphi-\vdagger\rVert_{\mathcal H}$ in the **RKHS** norm; `balance_gram()` and `Ku` both use a **Gaussian** kernel, whose RKHS is essentially analytic functions, so an LF iterate is not in it and the second factor is infinite. **The same failure mode §15 already diagnosed for the derivative parameterisation, reappearing in a stronger norm.** Proposed fix: Matérn/Sobolev with smoothness below the source-condition smoothness, plus an interpolation bound. Affects code and theory together.
**Finding 3 — `cor:trend` follows from A2, not A3**, against plan §7's expectation. A3 is a $p\times p$ local-rank condition and can hold while the linear direction sits in $\ker\mathcal A_{\mathrm{cond}}$, forcing A2 to fail when $E[\Delta Z\Delta X]\neq0$. Makes the corollary stronger than anticipated and **redirects the Stage 3 probe to test A2** — §7 probe (iii) is misdirected as written.
**Organising insight:** six of nine targets are statements about one functional $\nu_g(x)=E[g|X_2=x]f_2(x)-E[g|X_1=x]f_1(x)$; `rem:nophifree` and `prp:orthog`(i) are *the same equation* — the impossibility result is the setup for the orthogonal moment, not a side remark.
**Further corrections to the contract:** obstacle 4 was misplaced (no indicator in $\psi$; the interchange is in `lem:adjoint`, and the density needing continuity is that of $\Delta Y$ given $(X_1,X_2,\Delta Z,W)$, so **`assapp2` as cited is insufficient**); $\mathcal T$ must be **compact**, not merely of positive measure; obstacle 6 restated — the claim needing proof is the **strict inclusion** $\mathcal R(\mathcal A^\ast_{\mathrm{cond}})\supsetneq\mathcal R(\mathcal A^\ast_{\mathrm{uncond}})$, which §17.3 asserts without proof and which is the central technical argument for the conditional route; $\mu$ **drops out of the $\theta$-side** once `lem:bierens` gives density of the closed span, closing §14's open question there and leaving a growing family needed only on the $\varphi$-side as a numbered identification assumption.
**Scope recommendations adopted into plan §18:** `prp:effbound` scoped to the Severini–Tripathi analogue plus optimal-instrument-within-class from the start, full attainment declared open against Poirier; Stock–Wright S-set built in from the start rather than as a robustness afterthought; `thm:rootn` restated as a rate theorem with $\sqrt n$ normality as a conditional corollary; $\hat\theta$ defined as a **one-step** estimator since the moment is nonlinear in $\theta$ and A3 is only local; tangential-cone verification capped at one round because §2.6 dominates it.
**Escalations resolved by the orchestrator, recorded in plan §18:** (1) six second-order terms → §2.6 rewritten, A2′; (2) `prp:phirate` added to §2.7 as a new target; (3) §15 sieve vs `orthogonal_theta.R` RKHS conflict → resolved in favour of **RKHS**, per the user's 2026-07-28 decision, with §18.2 recording that the kernel must change to Matérn.

### 2026-07-28 13:05 — strategist-critic (proof-strategy review, round 1)
**Phase:** Strategy (HIGH severity) · **Gate:** 80
**Score:** **65 / 100 — DOES NOT CLEAR.** `/prove` not dispatched.
**Category scores:** conjecture clarity 12/15, target-object fit 11/15, toolkit 9/15, lemma sufficiency 15/25, obstacle realism 14/20, fallback design 4/10.
**Two CRITICAL findings, both of which I had already propagated into the plan:**

**C-1 (−10) — the remainder analysis is in the wrong norm; the $n^{-1/4}$ wall is over-stated.** The $s>a+\tfrac12$ threshold is arithmetically correct *for the $L^2$ norm*, but $L^2$ is not the natural norm. Under A2, $\nu=\mathcal A^\ast_{\mathrm{cond}}\ell_\dagger$, so $\langle\nu,h\rangle=\langle\ell_\dagger,\mathcal A h\rangle\le\|\ell_\dagger\|\|\mathcal A h\|$ — a **weak-norm** pairing, which is the Severini–Tripathi mechanism and the reason A2 is a *range* condition. In the weak norm mild ill-posedness needs only $s+a>\tfrac12$, and the severe case is **nearly parametric, not logarithmic**. The critic supplied the decomposition (verified by the orchestrator): with $(\Delta h)^2=\Delta(h^2)-2h(X_1)\Delta h$ and $q=E[\widetilde W\partial_u\ell_\dagger|X_1,X_2]$, $Q(h)=\tfrac12\langle\partial_u\ell_\dagger,\mathcal A(h^2)\rangle-E[q\,h(X_1)\Delta h]$ — **one of two pieces is weak-norm-able and free.**
**Two levers the memo missed.** (i) $\|\partial_u\ell_\dagger\|_\infty$ is a **design choice, not a primitive**: $\mathcal A^\ast_{\mathrm{cond}}$ maps an infinite-dimensional space onto functions of a scalar, so $\ker\mathcal A^\ast$ is infinite-dimensional and solutions form the affine set $\ell_\dagger+\ker\mathcal A^\ast$; validity of $\psi$ holds for *any* $\ell$ (mean-zero comes from A1 + N1). The binding constant can be minimised over the solution set — the ridge currently picks minimum-$\mathcal H_u$-norm, not minimum-$\|\partial_u\ell\|$. (ii) the variance half of $Q$ is a **trace, not a norm**, so the MISE-optimal stopping rule is not the remainder-optimal one.
**Elegant corollary:** `rem:nophifree` *forces* $\partial_u\ell_\dagger\not\equiv0$ — a constant-in-$u$ $\ell$ gives $L=c(z)u$ and hence a $(W,\Delta Z)$-measurable weight, which R2 forbids. **The impossibility result is exactly what creates the quadratic remainder.**
**Plan §2.6 amended** with a "severity under review" block; the scoping decision is suspended pending the sharp calculation.

**C-2 (−8) — tier T4 is invalid, and I accepted it into plan §18.4 without checking.** The memo claimed identification-robust inference needs "no A2, no rate condition." It does: an S-set's bias term vanishes **only by orthogonality, and orthogonality is A2**; without it the bias is $O_P(\sqrt n\delta_n)\to\infty$. Dropping A2 needs $\delta_n=o(n^{-1/2})$, **strictly stronger** than the $o(n^{-1/4})$ it was meant to insure against. All three repairs fail or backfire — a $\varphi$-free moment is ruled out by the paper's own `rem:nophifree`, and the profiled S-set needs exactly the entropy machinery `positioning.md` leg 3 says the paper avoids. **Plan §18.4 struck.**

**MAJOR findings:** M-1 §3.4 declares `prp:phirate` load-bearing for the Chen–Pouzo rebuttal while §3.3 recommends *assuming* it — a relocation, not a rebuttal. M-2 four missing lemmas, plus **the balancing solve at `orthogonal_theta.R:197` is itself a second Tikhonov-regularised ill-posed inversion**, contradicting §3.2's own argument against CGMM ("carrying two would make `thm:rootn` unprovable" — it carries two). M-3 the RKHS diagnosis blames the wrong factor (it is $\varphi_\dagger\notin\mathcal H_{\rm Gauss}$, not the LF iterate) and **the correct fix is the $L^2$ dual pairing, i.e. plan §15's sieve form, which the memo filed as a "retreat"** — this reopens the user's RKHS choice on technical grounds. M-4 "unconditional" is ambiguous; not well-posed under one reading, and under the other provable in two lines by **Jensen → operator ordering → Douglas's range-inclusion theorem (1966)**, with strictness via **Picard's criterion** — neither tool identified. M-5 §1.0's "exactly when" overstates necessity.
**Independently verified by the critic:** the Gaussian-kernel claim (`orthogonal_theta.R:157`, `:190`), the `cor:trend`↔A2 chain (and it is *stronger* — A3 involves $\ell_\dagger$, which does not exist when A2 fails, so "A3 holds while A2 fails" is **vacuous**), the `assapp2` insufficiency (density conditional on the *endogenous* regressors; §3.3 needs one more derivative than L-4 requests), and the $\varrho(1-\varrho)$ mechanism.

### 2026-07-28 14:00 — strategist (round 3, final round, COMPLETE)
**Verdict:** Refutation confirmed and independently verified rather than accepted. Error precisely diagnosed: taking $\phi=h$ in the $\nu_g$ identity applies the difference structure to **one** factor of $\Delta h$, leaving the other as a pointwise multiplier — so the period terms **add** into $\sigma_g$ instead of subtracting into $\nu_g$. That is the same accounting the memo used against C-1, not turned on its own construction.
**The sharpness result now carries an exact constant.** In $L^2(\pi)$ with $m:=M/\pi$ (which also resolves the m-12 measure mismatch), $\mathcal B_{\rm sym}=m\cdot-2\tilde{\mathcal K}$, and **Weyl's theorem** gives $\sigma_{\rm ess}(\mathcal B_{\rm sym})=\operatorname{ess\,ran}(m)$, hence $\sup_{\|h\|\le\delta}|Q(h)|=\tfrac12\delta^2\|\mathcal B_{\rm sym}\|\ge\tfrac12\delta^2\|m\|_{L^\infty}>0$. New target **`prp:sharpquad`**, stated with the constructive sequence leading and Weyl as the remark.
**The $M$ question: recorded OPEN as a well-posed feasibility problem** $\mathcal S\ell_\dagger\in\mathcal S(\ker\mathcal A^\ast_{\rm cond})$. Three carried observations: (i) no containment obstruction and the dimension count *favours* it — $\ker\mathcal A^\ast$ constrains the **difference** of period conditionals while $\mathcal S$ reads the **sum**; (ii) $M\equiv0$ is **necessary, not sufficient** — it removes the Douglas obstruction but leaves a genuine range condition, so it reopens the question not the rung, and $q\equiv0$ is the cleaner target since it kills $M$ and $\mathcal K$ together; (iii) the lever pays even when infeasible, because minimising $\|m\|_\infty$ minimises the **sharp constant**. SG-16 rebuilt on divergence along the test sequence in $k$ *and* mesh, since a discretised Picard check is meaningless for a non-compact operator.
**Leg 3 closed, and the claim narrowed honestly** — L-19 bounds uniformly over a **deterministic ball containing the LF path** (containment via $\varphi_m-\varphi_0\in\mathcal R(\hat{\mathcal A}^\ast)$ with norm polynomial in the cap): a **uniform LLN with a rate over a VC-subgraph class**, not a functional CLT. "We avoid empirical process theory entirely" is **withdrawn**, replaced by *"we do use an entropy bound; we do not use stochastic equicontinuity at the estimated point at $\sqrt n$ scale."*
**Dimension count fixed — and it explains plan §16.1.** $T$ is an unconditional expectation, so $d=1$ with no local-averaging denominator: $\hat\delta_n^{(T)}=O_P(n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log n})$, and order 8 at $h_u\asymp n^{-1/16}$ gives bias $n^{-1/2}$ — **matching the stochastic term exactly.** The binding component is $\hat\varrho$, costed for the first time at $O_P(n^{-r/(2r+d_Z)})$, requiring $r>d_Z/2$ with $d_Z=3$–$5$ in the application. **All four "falls short" clauses VERIFIED, and the one open question answered.** Model (1.2) is $Y_t-Y_{t-1}=\varphi(Z_t)-\varphi(Z_{t-1})+U_t-U_{t-1}$ under $E(U_t-U_{t-1}\mid W)=0$ — **purely nonparametric, no $Z'\theta$ anywhere.** Mechanical sweep: "partially linear"/"partly linear" **0**; "binary"/"discrete instrument" **0**; "parametric rate"/"root-n"/"$\sqrt n$" **0** — the only two "asymptotic normal" hits are in the *reference list*. Operator $(K\varphi)(w)=E(\varphi(Z_2)-\varphi(Z_1)\mid W=w)$: linear, conditional-mean, Tikhonov with $L^2$/Sobolev.
**Three structural correspondences found that the entry did not record — and they help the positioning rather than threaten it:**
1. **The same difference-of-densities adjoint.** Their Prop. 2.1: $(K^\ast\psi)(z)=\int\psi(w)\{f_{Z_2,W}-f_{Z_1,W}\}/\!\cdot\,dw$ — structurally this paper's $\nu_g$. The difference structure is shared machinery inherited from the differenced design; what is new here is that the operator is a **Fréchet derivative of a nonlinear map**, not a fixed linear conditional-expectation operator.
2. **Their non-identification condition is the linear-case analogue of `rem:nophifree`.** They note $K\varphi=0$ for *every* $\varphi$ when the law of $(Z_1,W)$ equals that of $(Z_2,W)$ — exactly Remark R2's tilted-marginals condition, reached independently in the linear setting. **Cite it: it converts R2 from a device of ours into a recognised feature of the differenced design.**
3. **Explicit singular system**, $K^\ast K\varphi_j=(\lambda_{2j}-\lambda_{1j})^2\varphi_j$, identified iff $\lambda_{2j}\neq\lambda_{1j}$ — a concrete precedent for H1's $\sigma_j\asymp j^{-a}$ in a differenced panel.
**LEAD FOR A1b(iii), the paper's most exposed flank.** Their **Proposition 2.2** gives identification when $(Z_1,Z_2)$ is *strongly identified* by $W$ and $Z_1,Z_2$ are *measurably separated* — a completeness-type primitive for exactly this differenced structure. **They then say "the assumption of Proposition 2.2 is too strong,"** which is itself useful: the honest primitive is hard even in the *linear* case, giving a citable precedent for recording the A1b(iii) gap rather than closing it cheaply. **Route to the theorist at Step 3.**

### 2026-07-29 02:15 — N-21 recorded as a standing rule: **checker authority is not build authority**
`references.bib` being authoritative for `/tools validate-bib` does **not** make it authoritative for `paper/main.tex`, which resolves against whatever `\addbibresource` names. A key living only in `references.bib` passes validation and **breaks the build** — silent at validation, loud at compile, which is the worst ordering. Rule: **before pinning any citation, check the key against the file `\addbibresource` names, not the file the validator reads.** This is the exact asymmetry the librarian's per-entry provenance is designed to expose from the other side, and it is what the round-6 `schumaker2007` pin walked into.

### 2026-07-29 03:45 — orchestrator (preamble.tex created and compile-verified) — **F-13 CLOSED**
**Target:** `paper/preambles/preamble.tex` (new). Resolves via `paper/latexmkrc`, which puts `./preambles//` on TEXINPUTS.
**F-13 was understated.** It was recorded against a copy outside this repository; **no `preamble.tex` existed here at all**, so every `\IR`, `\un`, `\upmodels`, `\vdagger` and every theorem environment in `quality_reports/theory/` and `paper/sections/theorems/` was undefined at document level. The four theorem files each declare "Requires `\IR`, `\un`, `\upmodels`, `\vdagger` from preamble.tex" in their headers — a contract with a file that was not there.
**The bug itself, and the answer to the open question.** `\newtheorem{remark}[]{Remark}` fails because an **empty** optional argument is not an absent one: LaTeX reads the bracket as "share the counter named here", finds no name, and errors rather than defaulting. And the standing question "are remarks numbered?" is now answered — **yes, numbered, sharing the result counter**, because the artifact requires it (below).
**Macro conventions established from the artifact, not guessed.** `\un` is a **bare symbol**, all 40 uses of the form `\un\{...\}` with literal braces, so defining it to take an argument would silently absorb the following token; `\upmodels` is a binary relation (rotated `\models`) with `\mathrel` spacing; `\vdagger` = $\varphi_\dagger$, macro-ised because the dagger/star distinction is load-bearing notation.

**COMPILING IT CAUGHT TWO DEFECTS I WOULD OTHERWISE HAVE SHIPPED.**
1. **`\cref` rendered every result as "theorem".** cleveref names a reference from the **counter**, not the environment, so the plain `\newtheorem{lemma}[theorem]{Lemma}` form makes `\cref{lem:adjoint}` print "theorem 12", and `\crefname{lemma}` cannot fix it because no reference is ever tagged `lemma`. **255 `\cref` calls across the theory files** (124 `lem:`, 71 `prp:`, 35 `cor:`, 25 `def:`, 6 `rem:`). Fixed with `aliascnt`: one shared sequence, references still carrying each environment's own name.
2. **Unnumbered remarks would have broken three live cross-references.** The theory files write `\cref{rem:R1}(iv)`, `\cref{rem:adjointreconcile}(iv)`, `\cref{rem:anchor}` — an unnumbered environment writes no counter, so `\label` captures the enclosing `\section` and these render "section 3(iv)": **silently wrong rather than an error.**

**RULE-VS-ARTIFACT CONFLICT, ESCALATED NOT RESOLVED.** `.claude/references/theorem-environment-style.md` specifies `\newtheorem*{remark}{Remark}` — unnumbered. The artifact contradicts it. The preamble numbers remarks because the cross-references demand it, and the discrepancy is **recorded in the file** rather than resolved: per `meta-governance.md` a reference is not edited without user approval. Either the reference is amended to allow numbered remarks in theory papers, or the three cross-references become prose. **User's call.**
**Assumptions left unnumbered by design** — they carry their own tags in the optional argument (`[A1c: First-order local identification]`), those tags are not a sequence (A1b, A1c, A1d, A2$'$, B1, C2$'$), and **they are never `\cref`'d: zero occurrences**. Proofs cite the tag, exactly as the style reference prescribes.

**VERIFICATION — the four real theorem files, not just a smoke test.** `lem-shiftinv`, `lem-bierens`, `lem-adjoint`, `rem-nophifree` compiled together: **exit 0, 0 errors, 0 undefined control sequences, 25 pages**, numbering in one shared sequence (Lemma 1, Remark 2, Lemma 3, Lemma 4, Corollary 5, Remark 6, ...). The 17 undefined cross-references are expected — each file declares dependencies that must appear earlier in a full document.

**THREE ENVIRONMENT GAPS FOUND, none fixed (they are not mine to change unprompted).**
1. **`siunitx` and `biblatex` are NOT INSTALLED** in this TeX Live (MacPorts, TeX Live 2026). Both are mandated by `working-paper-format.md`; `biber` **is** installed, so the missing half is the LaTeX side. Until `biblatex` is installed **no document in this repo can compile its bibliography**, and `\citet`/`\citep`/`\Citet` are undefined — the only undefined macros in the whole verification run. Install: `sudo port install texlive-latex-extra texlive-bibtex-extra` or `tlmgr install siunitx biblatex`.
2. **There is no `paper/main.tex`.** Nothing in this project has ever been compiled end to end. Not created here — it is the writer's, and Part F rendering is deliberately deferred.
3. **`instrumental.bib` does not exist.** Several notes record anchors (`hanke1995`, `engl2000`, `chenreiss2011`, `schumaker2007`) as "already in instrumental.bib"; the repo has only `Bibliography_base.bib` and two `references.bib`. ### 2026-07-29 08:10 — orchestrator (TeX toolchain resolved — **first successful end-to-end build in this project**)
**User correction, and it was right.** `siunitx`/`biblatex` were reported missing; they are present in **TinyTeX** (`~/Library/TinyTeX`, R's `tinytex`). My check had run against MacPorts TeX Live at `/opt/local/bin`, which is first on PATH and is therefore what `latexmk` invokes. **I reported one distribution's gap as the whole story.**

**Neither distribution alone can build this project** — the audit neither of us had:

| | MacPorts (`/opt/local/bin`) | TinyTeX |
|---|---|---|
| `siunitx`, `biblatex` | missing | present |
| `aliascnt`, `cellspace`, `dsfont`, `tabularray`, `titling`, `xurl` | present | missing |
| `biber` | 2.21 | 2.21 |

**Resolved (user chose TinyTeX):** `tlmgr install aliascnt cellspace tabularray titling xurl` plus **`doublestroke`** — `dsfont.sty` ships inside `doublestroke`, there is no package named `dsfont`, and `tlmgr` errors on the whole batch rather than the one bad name. All 37 required packages now resolve under TinyTeX.

**Shadowing fixed in `paper/latexmkrc`, not in the shell.** A guarded block prepends TinyTeX's `bin/*` when it exists, trying `$TINYTEX_ROOT` and the macOS/Linux/Windows roots, and does nothing when none do — so a machine with one complete TeX Live is unaffected and Overleaf ignores it. Repo-local, survives a fresh terminal, and benefits any forker using TinyTeX. **Verified working: `which xelatex` still reports the MacPorts binary before `latexmk` runs, and the build succeeds anyway.**

**BUILD RESULT — `latexmk` exit 0, 0 errors, 0 undefined control sequences, biber ran, 26 pages, `main.pdf` written.** Nothing in this repository had ever compiled end to end before.

### 2026-07-29 14:05 — theorist-critic (Step 4 review) — **80/100. PASSES THE COMMIT GATE.**
**0 CRITICAL · 3 MAJOR · 6 MINOR · −20.** Report `proofs_review_dispatch6.md`. Clears 80, below the 90 PR gate. **"No conclusion in Part G is wrong; every substantive claim I checked holds."** The three MAJORs are repairable in a short pass — two in one and three sentences, the third **in one character**.

**THE HIGHEST-RISK OBJECT WAS THE RIGHT ONE TO PRIORITISE, AND IT IS WHERE THE BIGGEST FINDING LANDED.** L-21 Step 2 **redefines** $\ell^\Pi_\star$ to be Step 1's continuous representative, then four lines later justifies the mollification's measurability "by Step 0" — **which established it for the pre-redefinition representative.** The mathematics is right, and the saving observation is exactly the one that makes the whole rebuild work: at each $t\in\mathcal T_\ast$ the two versions agree Lebesgue-a.e. in $u$, so **the interval average is identical for every $(u,t)$ — which is precisely why averaging works where the transform does not.** That observation is nowhere stated. ### 2026-07-29 18:20 — counterexample-hunter (two A6 boundary questions) — **NO CONFIRMED COUNTEREXAMPLE ON EITHER. Binary gate holds.**
Report `quality_reports/counterexamples/probe-results_a6.md`. **Relayed to the running fix pass mid-flight**, because both answers change what it is writing.

**Q1 is a formality, not a caveat.** $(1-\varepsilon_\varrho)^{n_{\rm m}}$ is correct, conservative, and — the structural point — **invariant to the first-stage construction**, since the bound uses only cross-fitting, overlap and iid-within-fold, *not* smoothness, series length or regularisation. Four adversarial constructions all fail: making the event material needs $\|\hat\varrho-\varrho\|\gtrsim\varepsilon_\varrho$, a **fixed constant**, which breaks A4(iii) outright. **The paper owes no caveat.**

**But two real findings inside Q1.** **(a)** The "probability one" leans on **cross-fitting**, not only overlap: drop it and estimate $\hat\varrho$ on the full sample by any interpolating rule, and $\hat\varrho(\Delta Z_i)=W_i$ for every $i$ a.s., so $\hat T\equiv0$ and **`prp:tccfails` is false with probability one — not on a null set.** `def:hatTexp` has the split; the proposition's own hypothesis list does not. **(b) THE REAL ONE, and it is the *partial* event, not the all-zero event.** For any series LPM the expected count of main-fold observations with $\widehat{\widetilde W}_i=0$ is $\asymp n_{\rm m}r_n^2=n^{1-2\alpha}$, which **diverges for every $\alpha<\tfrac12$** — and each such $i$ zeroes a row and column of $\mathbf G_n$. **With probability not tending to zero, `prp:tccrepair`(ii)'s nonsingularity hypothesis fails, `eq:tccrad` returns $\bar\rho_n=0$, and A6b's radius is vacuous** — so the file's sole demonstration that A6b is non-vacuous has no content there. **Captured by no stated assumption.**
**The planned truncation fix does more work than the review credited:** $|\widehat{\widetilde W}_i|\ge\varepsilon_\varrho$ **deterministically** for every $i$, which makes the "probability one" literally true with **no exception event at all** and removes the exact-zero mechanism in $\mathbf G_n$, restoring `prp:tccrepair`(ii).

**Q2 IS THE SUBSTANTIVE RESULT, AND IT CHANGES THE PAPER'S POSITIONING.**
**`prp:tccfails` does not survive restriction to $\mathcal S_n$ — and the obstruction is stronger than the inverse inequality the critic named.** The proof needs the spike to isolate one design point; on $\mathcal S_n$ the narrowest feature has width $\asymp L_x^{-1}$ against design spacing $\asymp n^{-1}$, and A8 caps $L_x\ll n^{1/2}$, so **every sieve feature contains $\gg n^{1/2}$ design points. The spike is not damped — it is not representable at all, by a margin of $n/L_x$.**
**The exact necessary condition ($\star$) was derived, and it is a *first-order* condition, not a curvature condition** — the quadratic remainder scales away. So A6-on-the-sieve is **not "the TCC restricted"** but *"$x$-smoothing acts as a near-identity on $\mathcal S_n$."* **`rem:tccwhy` survives verbatim**: the defect is still the pairing, not the constant and not the radius — what changes is that it becomes **finite** rather than infinite, and **what helps is $h_xL_x$, not $\bar\rho$.**
**The decisive finding: the block's own admissible window contains BOTH verdicts at ONE frontier-clearing configuration.** At $(\mu,s,a,\alpha)=(2,3,0.75,0.32)$, collecting A8, `eq:entropysieve`, (P-c), C5′(ii) and `lem:unifop` Step 4 gives $h_xL_x\in(n^{-0.24},n^{0.23})$ — tuning A ($h_xL_x\to0$) makes A6-on-sieve plausible, tuning B ($h_xL_x\to\infty$) makes it presumptively fail. **A6-on-the-sieve is genuinely open, and turns on a ratio no assumption constrains** — C5′ places no upper bound on $h_x$ at all. Establishing it needs a **new joint budget line $h_xL_x=O(\eta_{\rm TCC})$** (free at the sieve window's lower end, costing $\alpha(\frac{4\mu}{2\mu+1}-\frac1s)\ge\frac1{16}$ in general) **plus** a $\mathbf G_n$-conditioning bound — **the same $\lambda_{\min}$ object `prp:tccrepair`(iii) already declines to bound.**
**POSITIONING CONSEQUENCE, relayed as an instruction:** *"the file currently asserts reason (1) — the cone condition is false — as if unconditional, and on the sieve, which is where `def:lfstep` actually lives, it is not."* **Shift the weight to reason (3), saturation at $\mu=\tfrac12$, which is regime-independent, and restate (1) as "false on $\mathcal V$; on $\mathcal S_n$ contingent on $h_xL_x$."**
**And one cost independent of ($\star$):** even granting A6-on-$\mathcal S_n$, the referee's route is **projected** nonlinear Landweber — projection does not commute with $\hat T$, and Hanke–Neubauer–Scherzer needs the cone condition on an **ambient** ball containing $x^\dagger$, while $\vdagger\notin\mathcal S_n$. ### 2026-07-30 — theorist (CFF-2025 derivative-route FEASIBILITY ASSESSMENT, dispatched)
**The user answered the C1 question with a fourth option drawn from their own prior work**, not one of the three offered: *"it is well known that the LF algorithm has a saturation for nonlinear inverse problems … In Centorrino, Fève, Florens (2025) we took that into account by estimating the first derivative. **Located and read: `Centorrino,Feve,Florens(2025).pdf`, JoE 247 (2025) 105950 — the COMPANION PAPER**, already at D1 of `annotated_bibliography.md` under `centorrinofeveflorens2019` and in `frontier_map.md`'s FUNCTION-$\varphi$ column. **The device, §3 Eq. (12):** iterate on $\varphi'=D\varphi$ with $\varphi_j=D^{-1}\varphi'_j$ and $A_{\varphi_\dagger}:=T'_{\varphi_\dagger}D^{-1}$, integrating back to recover $\varphi$. The paper's own words: *"$D$ is playing the role of Hilbert scale, and the ill-posedness of the inverse problem and the smoothness of $\varphi_\dagger$ are going to be linked to the properties of $D$."*
**Why this plausibly dissolves C1 rather than patching it** — and the theorist is told to check the reasoning rather than assume it: **composing the scale into the operator makes the source condition spectral on $A=T'D^{-1}$ from the start, so there is no Hilbert-scale-to-spectral conversion to license and no Heinz range to respect.** It is also **exactly the preconditioned iteration the regularity-checker independently named as the canonical route above $\tfrac12$** — the checker called it "a different estimator", and the companion paper is that estimator. And it avoids the cost of the spectral-restatement option: $\mu$ stays linked to $D$'s properties, so H1's four-to-three parameter collapse survives.

**THE BLOCK IS ALREADY HALF-BUILT FOR IT, AND NOBODY NOTICED.** `lem:levels` (`results.tex:741–748`) defines $\mathcal D^{-1}_0:L^2(\pi)\to\mathcal V$ as integration from the left endpoint and **proves it bounded and injective, with $\mathcal D$ unbounded**; `results.tex:644` already calls $\mathcal D^{-1}$ *"the integration operator of the derivative parameterisation"*; `:603` identifies the kernel $\mathbf 1(x\ge v)$ as $\mathcal D^{-1}$. **And `lem:levels` has ZERO consumers — proved in an early round and never used.** The assumption-auditor had it in its matrix as an A0 consumer without remarking that nothing consumes *it*.

**Four transfer questions put to the theorist, with the third given veto power.** (1) Does $D^{-1}$ compose with the **differencing**, given $\varphi$ enters as $\Delta\varphi$, and does the left-endpoint normalisation interact correctly with $\mathcal V$'s centring? (2) Does it survive the **sieve**, where $D^{-1}$ is exact and raises spline order by one — help, hurt, or a change to A8? (3) **Does it disturb `prp:orthog` and the $\sqrt n$ result for $\theta$? "If this breaks the $\theta$ result, the device is unavailable however well it fixes C1 — say so plainly."** (4) What becomes of `eq:muhalf`'s $\mu>\tfrac56$, given CFF buys one derivative and **records an additional saturation effect on $\beta$** — it must be re-derived under the new operator, not assumed to survive.

### 2026-07-30 — theorist (CFF derivative-route assessment) — **VERDICT: THE DEVICE DOES NOT TRANSFER. Recommend the spectral restatement.**
Report `quality_reports/theory/panel-npiv-discrete/derivative_route_assessment.md`. No artifact edited, per the dispatch.

**The decisive reason, and it is independent of everything else: the device pushes the wrong way.** **CFF's derivative parameterisation is a remedy for a saturation CEILING. This paper has none** — `def:lfstep` runs **linear** LF, and (L5) already says *"the exponent $2\mu/(2\mu+1)$ is available for every $\mu>0$."* **`eq:muhalf` is a FLOOR**, arising from (P-f)'s remainder domination. Composing $\mathcal D^{-1}$ makes the operator **more** ill-posed, $a\mapsto a+1$, and *lowers* the index:
$$\mu_{\rm lev}=\tfrac{s}{2a}\ \longrightarrow\ \mu_{\rm der}=\tfrac{s-1}{2(a+1)}.$$
**Verified independently: $\mu_{\rm lev}-\mu_{\rm der}\propto s+a>0$, so the drop is STRICT for every $s,a>0$.** At `rem:h6bandwidth`(5)'s admissible $(s,a)=(3,\tfrac34)$, **$\mu$ falls from $2$ to $4/7\approx0.571$ — failing `eq:muhalf`'s $\mu>\tfrac56$** — and its `eq:frontier` would demand $r>22.5$ at $d_Z=3$. **And the formula reproduces CFF's own $\beta=(s-1)/(a+1)=8/7$ exactly**, so the arithmetic is checked against the source paper rather than against itself. `eq:muhalf` was re-derived rather than assumed and **survives verbatim**, since (P-f)'s chain runs entirely in $(\kappa,\alpha,\beta_{\rm bw})$.

**Two further reasons.** It would not close C1 by any mechanism the restatement lacks — **C1 dissolves because the condition is stated spectrally on the operator regularised, and $\mathcal D^{-1}$ contributes nothing to that** — and **CFF assumes 4.3 (spectral) and 4.4 (link) SEPARATELY**, so CFF does not escape the two-sided translation either. It also does not close A3, because the adjoint of a restriction maps into the restricted subspace in both parameterisations, so (L2) survives verbatim **and so does its defect**.

**What is favourable is real, and it relocates the failure.** `prp:orthog` and `thm:rootn` are **not** broken structurally: at $h=\mathcal D^{-1}_0h'$ the derivative is $-\langle(\mathcal D^{-1}_0)^\ast(\nu/\pi),h'\rangle$, and injectivity plus range-density — **already proved at `proofs.tex:854–858`** — leaves `eq:orthphi` unchanged; and $\Delta$ annihilates constants, so `lem:levels`'s left-endpoint normalisation is **void** for the composed operator. **So the device fails not by breaking $\theta$ but by failing to supply `thm:rootn`'s input.** It also explains `lem:levels`'s zero-consumer status: **all four clauses are consumed by the device and none by the levels parameterisation.**

**MY FRAMING OF OPTION B's COST WAS WRONG.** I told the user it costs H1's four-to-three parameter collapse. **It does not:** H1's matching is about $\mathcal A_{\rm cond}$ on $\mathcal V$, while (P-a) concerns a **finite-rank realised operator on $\mathcal S_n$ at a random base point** — so **(P-a) was already assuming its own matching, and B makes an existing assumption visible rather than adding one.** Further, **Option A = Option B + a proviso**, so B is strictly weaker and needs strictly less proved, while A preserves $\mu=s/(2a)$ intact but stacks an unverifiable alignment condition on Q-4 and adds a third constant to pin. **Recommendation: B.**

**A1/A2 sequencing answered — BOTH radii, and they are one symbol today.** $\mathsf R$ is an $H^s$ radius; $\mathsf R_\ast$ is a **spectral pre-image norm** entering $\mathsf c_{17}=2c_\mu\mathsf R^{1/(2\mu+1)}$ hence $\mathsf c_{15}$, which `def:lfstep` prints before the data are seen. **One symbol on the two sides of an inequality `def:lfstep` must establish — the same species as dispatch-8 MAJOR 2.1.** Order: restate (P-a) spectrally on the compression; split the radii in the same pass; then A1/A2.

**REFUSAL 1, and I verified it: my proposed $o_P$ repair was WRONG.** I gave it $P(|X_n|>\epsilon a_n)\le P(M_n>\epsilon)+E[P(|X_n|>M_na_n\mid\mathcal G)]$. It replied that the form is *true* but **neither site has its hypothesis**, because reaching $P(|X_n|>M_na_n\mid\mathcal G)\to0$ at a *single moving threshold* with $M_n=o_P(1)$ needs a **rate** for the conditional second moment, and `lem:epterm` supplies $o_P(1)$ with none. **Checked: at the actual sites $M_n$ is a constant multiple of the $L^2$ norm, so conditional Chebyshev returns $V_n/M_n^2=C^{-2}$ — a constant, not $o(1)$. The second term does not vanish.** What it wrote instead uses what Chebyshev/Markov *actually* deliver — a bound at **every** $\epsilon$ with a $\mathcal G$-measurable numerator: if $P(|X_n|>\epsilon a_n\mid\mathcal G)\le V_n\epsilon^{-\mathrm{exp}}$ a.s. for all $\epsilon>0$ with $V_n=o_P(1)$, then $X_n=o_P(a_n)$ — proved by truncation, $P(|X_n|>\epsilon a_n)\le P(V_n>\eta)+\eta/\epsilon^{\mathrm{exp}}$, which is cleaner than my sketch and gives an explicit finite bound. ### 2026-07-30 — theorist (specs for Part I) — **five written, all MATCH, all falsification predictions confirmed.**
Suite **112 → 117: 115 MATCH, 2 by-design MISMATCH, 0 crash** — and the honest form is *115 MATCH of which one is a retirement record* (`phirate-operator-noise-budget`, subject (P-b) struck). **I ran 12 of its 22 predicted perturbations and 12 of 12 flipped to MISMATCH**, including the decisive one: `rootn-frontier-implies-rndelta` with `claimed_end: 1/(4*mu)` **flips**, so the spec actively **refutes** the round-10 error rather than merely failing to support it.

**IT CONTRADICTED MY BRIEF, AND IT IS RIGHT.** I relayed the critic's requirement that `rootn-rate-products` carry an arm "at $\kappa\alpha=\tfrac14$ where **three of the four** must fail." Its tally, which I verified symbolically: at the boundary **exactly ONE of the four fails.** $\sqrt n\,\delta_n^2=(\ln n)^{2\kappa}$ **diverges** — fails. $r_n\delta_n$ **holds**, with power of $n$ exactly $-\frac{1-\kappa}{4\kappa}=-\frac1{8\mu}<0$ — *the margin identity reappearing inside the tally, and still strictly positive at the boundary where it is attained.* The other two carry **free** exponents $\beta_e,\beta_L$ and are **undecidable from primitives**, their conditions (A5(iv), (R-c.2)) remaining available at the boundary. Its ruling: the critic's count is right under the reading *"fail to be certified from the primitives"* — which is `cor:ratefloor`(a)/(b)'s own split — and **wrong under the $o(n^{-1/2})$ reading**. It adopted the first, said so in full, and gave the reason: *"a count carried in a sentence is precisely the species that produced the $1/(4\mu)$ error."*

**Design choices worth keeping.** In `rootn-log-absorption` it declared `eps` **real, not positive**, deliberately: `eq:frontier` is $\varepsilon>0$, so declaring it positive *"would put the boundary outside the identity's domain and certify only the half that never failed."* With `eps` real the $\varepsilon=0$ failure is obtained **by substitution into a certified identity** rather than narrated. Same move in `rootn-prelim-threshold`, where `c` is real so $c=0$ is in-domain and the $o$-versus-$O$ distinction is a substitution — and the arm at $\delta_n\asymp n^{-1/4}$ is not "the bound stops being useful" but *"the limit is $c^2$, a number the harness computes."* Two further arms at $n^{-1/2}$ and $n^{-1/3}$ **bracket the threshold from below**, so the spec discriminates *which* exponent is critical rather than merely that some exponent is.

**`rootn-jacobian-sign` covers the link that was uncovered.** The suite already had the derivative (`jacobian-scalar-chain`) and the convention (`jacobian-sign-convention`); missing was the **propagation** — that the minus in $\partial_\theta\bar\psi_n=-\hat J$ is what makes the plus in `eq:onestep` *cancel* the preliminary error rather than double it. It instantiated $\bar\psi_n$ as a quadratic so $\hat J$ is **derived** rather than posited and $\Psi^\theta_n$ is **generated** with its $\tfrac12$ and its sign. And it recorded what a sign error does: the preliminary error **doubled**, so $\sqrt n(\hat\theta-\theta_\dagger)$ diverges at every admissible configuration — *"invisible in the limit law, visible immediately in a simulation."* Exactly what the concurrent Monte Carlo needs.

**On the demoted spec it agreed after reading `lem:biasexp` rather than taking the critic's word — with a carve-out.** Agreed, and for a stronger reason than "easy by hand": **bi-affineness is the hypothesis that makes the vanishings automatic**, so a bi-affine surrogate confirms them by construction — it would test the surrogate. But it named one sub-claim that is *not* vacuous under that surrogate, because bi-affineness in $(L,\varrho)$ says nothing about the $u$-argument of $\hat\ell$, which is where the constant lives: for $\hat\ell$ affine in $u$, $\Psi^{(1)}_k=-\tfrac{l_1}2E[\widetilde W(\Delta h_k)^2]$, making explicit the $\tfrac12$ that feeds the $\tfrac12\to2\bar c_\pi$ chain. **Named `rootn-psi1-halfconstant` and deliberately not written**, since nothing this round moved it — the right spec to write if a later dispatch changes $\psi$ or the FTC step.

**Harness dependency declared so a future failure is diagnosable rather than mysterious:** specs 2 and 4 need `expand`'s `power_base` pass to split $(n^{-\alpha}\ln n)^\kappa$, which fires only because both factors are declared **positive** — hence `Ln` as a positive symbol rather than `log(n)`. If SymPy ever returns an unevaluated `Pow`, the residual shows the unsplit bracket: a harness outcome, not a refutation. Both files say so.

### 2026-07-30 — simulation-engineer, `thm:rootn` Monte Carlo — **VERDICT: "Matches".** Plus one verified theory finding.
Summary `quality_reports/sim/sim_thm_rootn_summary.md`; 7 scripts, 9 tables, 4 figures, 4 raw `.rds`. Reproducible: `Rscript scripts/simulations/sim_thm_rootn_run_all.R` (~2.5 h, 8 cores).

**The verdict is correctly conditioned.** At the remainder budget **the theorem's own hypotheses require** ($\sqrt n\delta_n^2$ small, realised $\le2.9$), every quantity is within 2 MC SE at $n=181{,}019$ for both DGPs and every gap shrinks monotonically: worst quantile deviation 1.58 / 1.65 MC SE, 95% coverage 0.944 / 0.946 (MC SE 0.005), $\hat{\rm sd}/{\rm sd}_\infty=1.022$, $\|\hat V-V\|_\infty$ falling $1.14\to0.10$. At $c_\delta=1$ gaps of 3.9–5.2 MC SE persist — **and it correctly declines to call that a mismatch**, because there $\sqrt n\delta_n^2=46.7$, so **A5(ii)'s second half is numerically false and the theorem's hypothesis does not hold.** That is the practical caveat, not evidence against the theorem.

**The Jacobian sign is clean:** central differences match $-\hat J$ to $1.1\times10^{-6}$ relative, sign agreement 1.000 in all 16 entries, gap to $+\hat J$ exactly 2.000; the one-step contracts to 0.080–0.560 with $+\hat J^{-1}$ and **expands to 2.00–2.05 with $-\hat J^{-1}$** — the doubling the spec predicts, observed.

**FINDING 1 — ESCALATION TO THE THEORIST. $J=E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']$ exactly, under A1. I verified this analytically and it holds.** `results.tex:3978` states, in italics, *"$J$ is the matrix of A3 and it is **not** $E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']$."* But $J:=E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']+E[\widetilde W\ell_\star(\Delta U,\Delta Z)\Delta Z']$, and the second summand vanishes identically: **A1 is $\Delta U\upmodels W\mid\Delta Z$**, so given $\Delta Z$ the factor $\widetilde W$ is $\sigma(W)$-measurable and $\ell_\star(\Delta U,\Delta Z)$ is $\sigma(\Delta U)$-measurable, hence conditionally independent, hence $E[\widetilde W\ell_\star\mid\Delta Z]=E[\widetilde W\mid\Delta Z]\,E[\ell_\star\mid\Delta Z]$ — and **$E[\widetilde W\mid\Delta Z]=0$ by construction**, since the glossary defines $\varrho:=E[W\mid\Delta Z]$ and calls $\widetilde W=W-\varrho(\Delta Z)$ *"conditionally centred."* The vanishing holds **for every $\ell$**, not just at $\ell_\star$. Measured: $\max|E[\widetilde W\ell_\star\Delta Z']|=2.2\times10^{-3}$ against MC SE $8.9\times10^{-4}$, $\max|t|=2.46$ over 16 entries where the expected max of 16 standard normals is 2.08 — consistent with zero.
**Consequences it names: A3 is REDUNDANT given A1 + A1b(iv)** — and the `assumptions.tex` comment calling A3's matrix "a DIFFERENT matrix" from A1b(iv)'s is wrong — **and `lem:momzero`(iii)'s second-derivative envelope bounds zero.** This touches the maintained block, so it goes to the theorist and the assumption-auditor, not to me.

**FINDING 2 — the log-log grid earned its keep, and quantifies the dispatch-10 amendment.** $\sqrt n\delta_n^2=n^{-0.012}(\ln n)^{1.6}$ runs $11.7\to46.7$ across the grid — **increasing everywhere**, turning over only at $\ln n=133$ ($n\approx10^{58}$) and dropping below 1 at $\ln n=908$ ($n\approx10^{394}$). **A power grid shows a flat line and hides this.** The amendment is worth a factor of **12 to 54**: the log-free reading gives 0.87–0.94 where the amended symbol gives 11.7–46.7. Its conclusion: **`cor:ratefloor`, not `thm:rootn`, is what describes any feasible $n$.**

**FINDING 3 — `eq:lfsched` is not scale-invariant, and `prp:stopfeas`'s factor counts only part of the cost.** Measured caps 321,707 / 1,266,677 / 4,476,070 at $n=500/2000/8000$ against $\hat\delta_n^{-2}=54/130/315$ — ratios 5,958 / 9,744 / 14,210. The **rate** part is $\approx8$ at $n=2000$, inside `prp:stopfeas`'s 5–50; the remaining $\approx1{,}200$ is the **constant prefactor of `eq:normconst`**, since $\omega_n/\|\hat{\mathcal A}|_{\mathcal S_n}\|=640$–$926$. At $\mathsf c_{14}\le10^{-2}$ the discrepancy rule **saturates the cap in 100% of replications**; at $\mathsf c_{14}=1$ it stops at $m=1$ always; the transition spans one decade. **Lepskii never saturates** ($\hat m^{\rm Lep}/\bar m_n=0.37/0.24/0.13$), carries no unidentified constant, and beats the discrepancy rule wherever that rule saturates (0.331 vs 0.537 at $n=8000$). Condition numbers reported as asked: compression $6$–$7\times10^{13}$, Gram $10^{33}$–$4\times10^{33}$ — two different objects, and the file is right to separate them.

**Limits declared without prompting, which is the standard I asked for.** The realised operator's singular values fit an **exponential** better than a power law ($\hat a\approx3$ against H1's $\tfrac34$), so the φ-step arm's *rate* comparison is **off-hypothesis** — the limit-law arm does not use H1 and is unaffected. Nuisances are **injected** at the rates `eq:foldrates`/A4(iii)/(R-a) price, because $\hat\ell$ is "constructed nowhere" and $\hat\varphi^{(0)}$ has no supplier (Q-1): *"This tests the theorem in its own terms and does not test whether any estimator attains those rates."* All twelve such choices are declared as D1–D12 in the script headers. And its "rough" $h$-direction turned out **more benign** than "smooth", so D3c did not achieve the adversarial geometry it was designed for — reported as a failed design rather than quietly dropped.

**Two seeding defects it found and fixed mid-run**, both of the kind that silently destroys a Monte Carlo: a `set.seed()` **inside a parallel worker**, which froze the preliminary's error direction; and `mclapply` not advancing the parent L'Ecuyer stream between cells, so **every cell would have shared child streams**.

And what is *not* touched: $\Sigma=E[\psi_\star\psi_\star']$ does not vanish, and Neyman orthogonality concerns **nuisance** directions, not $\partial_\theta$ — so the debiasing story should be untouched; confirm or correct.

### 2026-07-30 — theorist (dispatch 13) — **$J=E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']$ UPHELD. A3 retired. But not on my route.**
**It upheld the conclusion and rejected my argument for it**, which is the outcome I asked for. My route was conditional-independence factorisation given $\Delta Z$; it points out that route *"does more work than needed, and it is the route most exposed to your own first objection."* **The shorter route was already in the file:** `lem:momzero` Step 2, four lines above the false sentence, proves from A1's first clause alone that $P(W=1\mid\Delta U,\Delta Z)=\varrho(\Delta Z)$, hence $E[\widetilde W\mid\Delta U,\Delta Z]=0$ — convention **(P4c)**. Then pull the $\sigma(\Delta U,\Delta Z)$-measurable factor out by the **tower property**. The distinction is the point: *"factorisation reasons about the law, the tower step reasons about measurability. Your route is the vulnerable one; the file gets the other."*

**Three sites already contained the refutation and nobody evaluated them at the right argument.** `lem:nusigma`(v), proved since dispatch 1: $E[\widetilde W\,\Xi(\Delta U,\Delta Z)]=0$ **for every integrable $\Xi$**. `rem:rootnhyp`(1), in Part I itself. `prp:orthog`(ii), running the identical argument for the $L$-direction, endorsed in writing by the round-3 critic. **Nobody set $\Xi(u,z)=\ell_\star(u,z)z'$.** Its verdict: *"a bookkeeping failure, not a mathematical one."*

**All four of my failure modes came back negative, and one of them I had backwards.** Selection: no — $\ell_\star=\mathcal W\ell^\Pi_\star$ is a fixed Borel map, and *"a law-dependent choice of a deterministic function is still a deterministic function"*; measurability is a property of the map's **arguments**, of which $W$ is not one. N1: no, twice. Integrability: free, and **verbatim the bound Step 3 already used**. **Truncation: I was right about $\hat J$ and it changes nothing about $J$** — `def:moment` writes $\widetilde W:=\widetilde W(\varrho)$ *at the true propensity*, so $\widehat{\widetilde W}^{(-k)}$ is **not** centred, `eq:Jhat`'s second summand does **not** vanish, and `lem:sandwich` Step 3 pays its cost unchanged.

**THE HISTORY, which is the more useful finding.** Round 1 routed identification of $\theta_\dagger$ through A3 — wrong. Round 2's M-3 repaired that via A1b(iv), giving as ground that A3 is *"nonsingularity of a DIFFERENT matrix … and is itself conditional on A2."* **The conditionality half is right and survives; the "different matrix" half was asserted and never computed.** Dispatch 10 displayed $J$ for the first time and hardened the uncomputed half into an italicised claim inside a definition. **"A warning about conditionality became a false claim about identity across three rounds."** Diagnosed as a σ-field scope slip of exactly the species (P4b)/(P4c) were split to police — and it names the confusable object: $q=E[\widetilde W\partial_u\ell_\star\mid X_1,X_2]$ genuinely does **not** vanish, and a reader carrying $q$ in mind reads $E[\widetilde W\ell_\star\Delta Z']$ as non-vanishing too. Now on $q$'s glossary row.

**A fourth consequence I did not claim, and it flags it hardest:** `def:onestep`'s ground for the one-step read *"A3 is a local rank condition … and no global rank condition is available."* **The population half is now false** — a global rank condition *is* available, it is A1b(iv), and $\theta_\dagger$ is the unique root of the population moment on all of $\mathbb R^p$. The operational conclusion survives (at *estimated* nuisances $\bar\psi_n$ is genuinely non-affine) but was argued from the wrong object.

**Four gains, and one is live for Step 8.** A3 becomes a **theorem** — it was the only assumption in the block stated conditional on another assumption. $J$ is symmetric PSD and is the *same* matrix that identifies $\theta_\dagger$ and normalises the preliminary. The population moment has a **unique global root**, strictly stronger than A3 offered. And **$\Sigma$ splits exactly** (`eq:sigsplit`), the cross term dying by the same tower step, so the sandwich is **the oracle plug-in variance plus a PSD term** — the debiasing buys removal of the $\langle\nu/\pi,h_k\rangle$ bias and **is paid for in variance**. Live for `prp:effbound`.

**Both of my conjectures confirmed, with a sharpening I did not have.** $\Sigma$ does not vanish — *"it is quadratic in $\widetilde W$ where $J$ is linear."* Orthogonality untouched **and it must be, or the block collapses**: $\Delta h$ is $\sigma(X_1,X_2)$-measurable, **not** $\sigma(\Delta U,\Delta Z)$-measurable, so $E[\widetilde W\ell_\star\Delta h]\neq0$ — indeed **A2 is precisely the assertion that it equals $-E[\Delta Z\Delta h]$**, and were it zero for every $h$ one would get $\nu_{\Delta Z}\equiv0$, *the instrument does not move the regressor.*

**Six refusals, all argued.** It did not replace $\hat J$ by $\mathbb E_n[\Delta Z\Delta Z']$ — tempting and consistent, but it breaks the exact identity $\partial_\theta\bar\psi_n=-\hat J$ and would move `rootn-jacobian-sign`; **recorded as an option not taken rather than silently omitted.** It did not delete `lem:momzero`(iii)'s envelope (it is *true*, marked vacuous in place); did not strike A2′(i.b), A2′(ii) or A2; did not write the simulation's fitted exponents into the `.tex`, only what it derived; **did not adopt my argument as given**; and **did not drop the transpose from $J^{-1}\Sigma J^{-1\prime}$ despite $J$ now being symmetric, because $\hat J$ is not.**

**Secondary item 2 DONE and re-derived rather than transcribed** — turnover at $n^\ast=\exp\{2\kappa/(2\kappa\alpha-\tfrac12)\}$, and *"your numbers check out exactly"*: at $\mu=2$, $\alpha=0.32$, $\beta=0.012$, $n^\ast=e^{400/3}\approx10^{58}$. Only the derived form went into the `.tex`. **Item 1 DEFERRED, not declined**, as open item Q-4, with a reason that is not budget: repairing it re-opens `def:lfstep`, *Part H, a different dispatch's contract*. One thing it states now: **`prp:stopfeas` is tuning-parameter critical in a way its text does not disclose** — a disclosure defect independent of the factor count.

### 2026-07-30 — orchestrator: the build broke, and a balance count could not have seen it
**The theorist reported "all environments and `\[…\]` pairs balanced (verified by count)". True, and insufficient — the build failed with 4 hard errors and no PDF.** Cause: `\begin{lemma}[… $J=E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']$]` — **the `]` inside `E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']` closes the optional argument at the first unbraced `]`**, leaving the `$` unmatched. **On the two findings already acted on:** the mathematics of $J=E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']$ is settled independently by the theorist, so what I want is narrower and more useful — **whether the simulation's evidence as reported supported the claim at the time, or whether a max-$|t|$ of 2.46 over 16 entries was over-read and got lucky.** And whether one decade of transition establishes `prp:stopfeas`'s tuning-parameter criticality.

**CRITICAL A — the φ-step tables cannot have been produced by the shipped script, and I confirmed it.** Every branch of `phistep_rep` bounds the stop by the cap, so $\hat m^{(1)}\le\bar m_n$ must hold in every replication. **Ran its command: five cells have ratio $>1$** (1.045, 1.060, 1.030, 1.008, 1.005). And its corroborating check came out **worse than it predicted**: `mbar_new` is `prelim`-free by construction and should vary by $<0.5\%$ across cells, but the measured spread is **1.22 / 1.16 / 1.05** at $n=500/2000/8000$ — it estimated 1.08 at $n=500$ from the tables alone. **The shipped `.tex` and `.rds` are not reproducible from the shipped `.R`.** Likely proximate cause it identified: `cap_mbar()` in `sim_thm_rootn_dgp.R` implements `eq:cap` with proportionality constant **1** while the live path uses a realised $\omega$ of 77.6–119.3 — and `cap_mbar()`/`band_hu()` are **dead code, defined and never called**. **Until re-run, no number from the φ-step arm may be quoted — including its stopping-rule finding.**

**CRITICAL B — the verdict paragraph is not supported by its own tables, and B1 is a correction I owe upstream.** The engineer wrote that at $c_\delta=1$ *"A5(ii)'s second half is numerically false, so the theorem's hypothesis does not hold"* — **and I relayed that to the user as sound.** It is false. **A5(ii)'s second half is $\delta_n=o_P(n^{-1/4})$, a statement about a SEQUENCE, invariant to any positive multiplicative constant.** Verified symbolically: at $(\mu,\alpha)=(2,0.32)$, $\kappa\alpha=0.256>\tfrac14$, and $\lim_n c_\delta\,\delta_n/n^{-1/4}=0$ **for every positive $c_\delta$**. **Both cells satisfy identical hypotheses; there is no principled ground to exclude $c_\delta=1$.** (The ratio nonetheless *rises* to a peak at $\ln n=400/3$, $n\approx10^{58}$ — which is the feasible-$n$ finding, and is why the finite-$n$ picture and the asymptotic statement disagree without contradicting.)
Also under B: **"every gap shrinks monotonically" is contradicted by the shipped table** — DGP-B's sd ratio bottoms out at $n=8{,}000$ and **rises over the last three grid points**; the worst quantile gap is non-monotone and is 2.64 SE at $n=8{,}000$, outside the engineer's own bar. **"Matches at the largest $n$" is partly an artefact of halving $B$ there**: $B$ drops 5000→2000 exactly at the two cells where the verdict is pronounced, inflating the sd-ratio SE from 0.010 to 0.016 — on the engineer's own 2-SE bar the sd ratio **fails at every $n\le22{,}627$**, and at $n=181{,}019$ **would have failed at 2.2 SE had $B=5000$ been kept.** And at $c_\delta=1$ the mismatch **grows** rather than persisting, while the summary reports only the *smallest* available mismatch measure in SE units (worst quantile, 3.9–5.2) and never converts the sd ratio (**11.7 SE**) or the 90% coverage shortfall (4.75 SE).

**Its answer to my Q1 — the injected-nuisance question — is the most useful thing in the report.** Not a tautology, and it says why: the theorem's content is that the four Step-4 products **exhaust** the remainder, and a missing term, a wrong $J$ or $\Sigma$, or a missing propensity correction would all have shown. The sharpest instance is real evidence — $\hat\varrho$ perturbed at $r_n=n^{-0.32}$, far slower than $n^{-1/2}$, with coverage staying nominal, is *"a genuine, falsifiable confirmation of `lem:sandwich`'s 'no Neyman correction for the propensity appears in it'."* **But three idealisations narrow the claim and only one is declared.** (a) **Every injected error is mean-zero in direction** — a real LF error carries a deterministic regularisation bias that does not average away, and the headline coverage and sd ratio are exposed where the linearisation residual is not. (b) **Every injected error lives in a low-dimensional span containing the truth** — $h$ spans only **three distinct frequencies** and $\varphi_\dagger$ *is* the first of them, while the φ-step arm's own measurement puts the operator's damped directions far beyond mode 6. **The two arms' findings are never connected, and that connection turns "D3c did not achieve its geometry" into "the injection basis was structurally incapable of reaching it."** (c) Full independence for complement-fold measurability — correct for the theorem as stated, but the exercise cannot verify cross-fitting does its job.

**On my narrower question about the $J$ finding: not over-read, but the design guaranteed the answer.** The DGP makes the identity true **by construction** ($\Delta U$ drawn with no dependence on $W$), so the measurement is an implementation check on `dgp_draw`, **not independent evidence about the population identity**. $\max|t|=2.46$ over 16 entries is a clean non-rejection at $p\approx0.20$. *"Did it get lucky? Somewhat"* — a 3.2 was within reach, and the correct reading would still have been "the algebra says zero, the MC is noisy". **The missing piece is power**: the pilot can exclude only a summand exceeding **0.24% of $\|J\|_\infty$**, never stated. Its net: the claim was carried by the one-line argument, which the summary states first and correctly and routed as *"none is a counterexample"* — **"that framing was right"** — but the simulation alone would not have supported retiring A3.

**On finding #2: the qualitative conclusion is stronger than claimed, the presentation is not.** The transition spans **two** decades with **one** interior grid point at which the statistic is a *mixture*, so the shape is unresolved — but the decisive evidence needs no finer grid: at that point the switch moves **25,121 → 4,204 → 1** across a 16× change in $n$, so **no fixed $\mathsf c_{14}$ can be $n$-stable**, which is stronger than "tuning-critical". **The Lepskii comparison is not fair as run** — it is made at a $\mathsf c_{14}$ where the DP saturates by construction, and no cell reports the DP at an intermediate $\mathsf c_{14}$.

**What it checked and found SOUND, which matters given the score.** **Both seeding defects are genuinely fixed in the shipped scripts** — no `set.seed()` on any parallel path, `nextRNGStream` + `mc.reset.stream()` correctly ordered in *both* files, and the stream advanced for checkpoint-skipped cells so a resumed run reproduces a fresh one. **The log-log grid rationale is backed by the resolution it claims** — $(\ln n)^{1/4}$ moves 27% against a ~1% SE, ~27 SE, *"verified, not a rationale without resolution."* **All budget arithmetic exact**, including the $10^{58}$ turnover and the 12.4–54.1 amendment factor. **MC SEs correct and stated before any gap is interpreted**, as I required. And a point in the exercise's favour the summary never makes: **the core grid runs the harsher of the two geometries.**

### 2026-07-31 — simulation-engineer fix round — **COMPLETE. Both CRITICALs closed; verdict held, not upgraded.**
~9.4 h of compute; all four arms re-run end to end from the shipped source in one detached execution, limit law at **constant $B=5{,}000$ across all 60 cells**.

**CRITICAL A closed, and I verified it: 36 cells, ZERO violations of $\hat m^{(1)}\le\bar m_n$, max ratio exactly 1** (was 5 of 30); cap spread **1.0064 / 1.0046 / 1.0011** (was 1.222 / 1.156 / 1.049); stage-1-saturating cells now agree **exactly** (was 9.5% apart).

**And the diagnosis refutes the hypothesis the critic offered and I relayed.** `cap_mbar()` was **not** the cause — it verified the refactored cap path is numerically identical to the round-1 inline expression (0.0e+00 difference on $h_u$ and $\omega_n$, identical integer cap). Its argument is better than the one we had: **no cap formula can produce that violation, because the bound is structural** — every branch returns 1, $\bar m_n$, or a bisection result inside $[1,\bar m_n]$. It is only possible if the **stored $\bar m_n$ is not the cap the loop used**, and the cause was **provenance: the checkpoint resumed on a *design* match alone, merging cells written by different code versions.** Fixed structurally rather than by patching the symptom — checkpoints now carry an **MD5 fingerprint of every replication-defining source file**, writes are atomic, and the invariants are asserted at runtime.

**B1 withdrawn, and sharper than either the critic's framing or mine.** I said A5(ii)'s second half is invariant to a positive constant. It read the file: **$c_\delta$ is the constant in A5(ii)'s *first* half and does not appear in the second half at all** — $\delta_n=o_P(n^{-1/4})$ is a property of a sequence the simulation computes identically in both cells. Both configurations satisfy identical hypotheses.

**B2 corrected and partly REVERSED.** Under constant $B$, **DGP-A is not monotone** ($1.0319\to1.0349$) — a new finding — while **DGP-B is monotone throughout: its round-1 non-monotonicity was the halved-$B$ artifact.** So the critic was right that monotonicity failed and wrong about which DGP. All measures now in SE units (`sim_thm_rootn_seunits.tex`): at $c_\delta=1$ the sd ratio is **18.85 SE** and 90% coverage **−9.18 SE**, against the **worst quantile gap the round-1 summary reported alone** — 2.7× smaller.

**The experiment I asked for bit, and found something neither of us predicted: the idealisation concealed a LOCATION bias, not a scale bias.** With a deterministic error component the sd ratio gets **better** (18.85 → 6.78 SE) and coverage marginally worse, but **centring — $\approx0$ in every mean-zero cell, max 1.95 SE — becomes 3.34 SE at $n=181{,}019$ and 5.51 SE at $n=1{,}000$.** Exactly the signature of a bias that does not average away, **on the statistic round 1 computed and discarded** (finding C1). The two findings interlock.

**And it measured the connection I asked it to state.** The injected error sits where the operator damps it by **3.7–4.9×**, against a resolvable dynamic range of $7.5$–$8.6\times10^6$ — **10% of the spectrum in log terms, and the gap widens with $n$.** *"Structurally incapable, not merely unachieved."*

**Three refusals, all specific, and one corrects me.** (1) **Finding D is wrong for the compression** — $\sigma_{\min}/\sigma_{\max}=1.16$–$1.33\times10^{-7}$, so $\kappa\approx8.6\times10^6$, **nine orders below $1/\varepsilon_{\rm mach}$**: a genuine measurement. I verified this: the critic computed $\kappa=(\max/\min)^2=1.1\times10^{33}$ and inferred $\max/\min=3.3\times10^{16}$, which is valid for the **Gram** — which *is* squared, and *is* at the floor, now marked $\ge$ — and does not carry across. (2) **"3 in the `.tex` versus 5 in the `.rds`" is not evidence of two datasets** — the `.tex` is faithful; Panel A printed one $\bar m_n$ per row. **I reported that contrast as if it showed two datasets; it does not, though the conclusion stands and the argument is now stronger.** (3) Finding E's sign test **weakens** at $c_\delta=0.25$ — 7 of 8, $p=0.070$, not 8/8 at $p=0.004$ — and is decisive only at $c_\delta=1$.

**Verdict HELD at "Matches at large $n$ only" — it did not upgrade itself after fixing both CRITICALs.** Its grounds: the sd ratio fails a 2-SE bar at every $n\le64{,}000$ in DGP-A, DGP-B's worst quantile is 2.92 SE at the largest $n$, and $c_\delta=1$ matches nowhere. Not "Persistent mismatch": the linearisation residual declines monotonically in **every cell of every panel including $c_\delta=1$**, $\|\hat V-V\|_\infty$ likewise, and the ablation reproduces $O(n^{-1/2})$ (ratios 2.86, 2.77 against $\sqrt8=2.828$).

### 2026-07-31 — regularity-checker, Part I — **55/100. 3 MAJOR, 5 MINOR, no CRITICAL. Nothing counterexample-shaped to route.**
Report `quality_reports/regularity/regularity-audit_partI.md`.

**MAJOR 1 — product measurability of the estimated nuisances is nowhere stated, and Part I DROPPED the one clause the block already had.** I verified both halves. `lem:balance` (`results.tex:1743`) requires *"$\hat\ell$ is $\sigma(\mathcal I_a)$-measurable with $\hat\ell\in L^2(P_{\Delta U,\Delta Z})$ almost surely"* — read as a **random element of $L^2$**, that delivers a jointly measurable representative. **`def:crossfit`(c) re-declares (P8)'s conditioning convention and does NOT re-declare this clause**, replacing it with the weaker per-realisation *"Borel, bounded, and continuous in $u$"*. Separate measurability in $\omega$ plus Borel in $(u,z)$ does not give joint measurability.

Three places it is load-bearing rather than decorative: **composition of a random function with random arguments** ($\hat\ell(\Delta U-\lambda\Delta h,\Delta Z)$ — continuity handles the $u$-slot only, nothing is given in $z$ or $x$); **hypotheses that presuppose the object is a random variable** — (R-a)'s $E\|\hat b\|^4=O_P(1)$ and `eq:foldrates` are *"probability statements about frozen integrals"*, and without product measurability they **are not random variables and the hypotheses are not well posed**; and **the freezing/substitution identity itself**, which every $E[\cdot\mid\sigma(\mathcal I_{-k})]$ in three lemmas uses and which the block never states. **And it answers my $V_n$ question directly: measurability of the numerator is "asserted at both sites, argued at neither"** — the argument is Tonelli, unavailable until the clause exists. I confirmed the corroborating sweep: **every "jointly measurable" in the corpus concerns a *deterministic* object** — $q$, $\ell^\Pi_\star$, the kernel, $G'_\varphi$ — and the file constructs those versions with real care (`proofs.tex:2355–2409` is an elaborate construction). It simply never did it for a sample-dependent one. **Repairable in one clause**, plus stating the freezing lemma once beside (P8).

**MAJOR 2 — (P8)'s transfer rule does not have the hypothesis its two Part I sites supply, and the hypothesis it does have is unattainable there.** Conditional Markov/Chebyshev delivers a bound at **every** $\epsilon$ with a $\mathcal G$-measurable $V_n$; at a threshold that is a fixed multiple of $V_n^{1/p}$ that bound is a **constant, not $o(1)$**, and driving it to zero destroys $M_n=O_P(1)$ unless $V_n$ carries a rate. **The header makes exactly this argument for the $o_P$ case and does not notice it applies verbatim to the $O_P$ case** — and the dispatch-11 change log confirms the diagnosis stopped at the conclusion, recording those two sites as *"UNTOUCHED"*. The mismatch is in the **hypothesis shape**, not the conclusion. Per-site: **`lem:sandwich` Step 2 over-reaches and does not need to** — the existing $o_P$ companion gives what Steps 4 and 6′ actually consume; **`lem:prelimtheta` Step 4 genuinely needs $O_P(\delta_n)$** and wants a third (P8) clause, two lines, *with a device the file already owns*. **Routing note it flagged without auditing: the same shape appears at the Part H sites the header names as transfer-rule consumers.**

**MAJOR 3 — $\|\hat\ell\|_\infty$ and $\|\partial_u\hat\ell\|_\infty$ are suprema of a random function over an uncountable index with no separability supplied.** Continuity is given **in $u$ only** — nothing in $z$ — so measurability of the supremum is not established and (R-a) is not a well-posed probability statement as it stands. Contrast drawn with the *population* object, where `cor:elldiff` gives boundedness *"uniformly in $z$"* and $\ell_\star$ is deterministic. Repairable in a line by declaring an essential supremum, with one owed check that the exceptional set is $P$-null since the envelope is used pointwise at the data.

**M1 answers my quantifier question, and the answer is that it cancels.** The companion is *stated* in the strong form (one null set, then all $\epsilon$) but its proof fixes a single $\epsilon$ and needs only the weak per-$\epsilon$ form; the sites *claim* the strong form but derive it from Chebyshev, which delivers the weak one. **"The over-claim at the sites and the over-demand in the statement cancel exactly."** Formal only.

**Three MINORs worth keeping:** conditional-versus-unconditional integrability of $\hat\psi^{(-k)}$, so the conditional expectations are strictly *generalised* ones; a **vector-valued mean value theorem** used at four sites where the MVT has no vector form — harmless, only an envelope is wanted, **but inconsistent with the file's own practice at `prp:frechet` Step 2, which refuses the Lagrange form on exactly these grounds**; and `prp:medianpart`'s conditioning σ-field when the partitions are *drawn* rather than fixed.
