### 2026-07-31 — theorist (dispatch 14) — **all four narrow audits actioned.** Build 288 pages, clean.
Verified: **exit 0, 288 pages (up from 273), zero hard errors, zero undefined refs, zero multiply-defined labels, zero unresolved citations; specs 115/2 unchanged; sync 23.**

**Both gate-blocking collisions closed.** $\psi$-as-CGF renamed to $\mathsf H(\lambda)$ with the slot swept and the ground cited to C26 — *"exactly C26's species one Part later."* $q$-as-partition-index renamed to $\mathsf p$, **the lowercase of its own count, an extension of C73 rather than a new register slot, so C1–C79 stays contiguous.** And the sweep found a **third live $q$** nobody had reported — a bound coordinate index in `lem:bierenscore` — plus an unseparated italic $Z_n$ in the same proof.

**Regularity closed, with a departure on the sup-norm that is better than the checker's phrasing.** A plain essential supremum over $(u,z)$ is **unsafe**, because every consumer evaluates the envelope at a *shifted* first argument whose law need not be dominated. The correct reading is a **genuine sup in $u$** (legitimate — continuity makes it a countable sup) and an **essential sup in $z$**, since the second argument is always a draw of $\Delta Z$. ### 2026-07-31 — orchestrator: two mechanical checks added, CHECK 7 and CHECK 8
**Rationale, and it is the measured result rather than a preference:** dispatch 14 was briefed on eight priorities across five files, and **two of three re-sweeps came back lower** because each repair carried its own defect class. The two classes that recur are exactly the ones a checker can catch, so I stopped hand-fixing them.

**CHECK 7 — symbolic-spec source pointers.** Every spec's `source:` is a claim about where a thing is, and this project moves text constantly. **77 of 117 specs cite a range that no longer contains the label they name.** Worse than the derivation-verifier's hand count because it checks every citation rather than a sample. Its own decisive argument for mechanising: **round 12 hand-repointed two of these and both are wrong again** — *"hand-repointing rots within two dispatches."*

**CHECK 8 — retired tags described as maintained. Getting this right took three calibration passes, and the calibration is the design.**
- **The first draft fired 31 times and about 29 were legitimate.** Refuting a clause requires *discussing* it, so A6 — which a whole proposition and four remarks exist to refute — produced twenty hits that **were the refutation itself**. A check that flags the proof of a retirement as a failure to record it is worse than no check, *"because it teaches its reader to skim"* — the warden's own standard: a sweep nobody trusts is a sweep nobody runs.
- **Narrowed to the shape the real defect has:** a **present-tense copula describing the tag as a kind of assumption** — "B1(vi) *is an* identification assumption." That is a claim about **current status**, which a retirement must sweep; discussion of a tag's falsity is not.
- **Then two of three known sites still escaped, because the phrase wraps mid-clause** — "is an identification" ends one line and "assumption" opens the next. Joining the following line is safe **here and only here**: it widens what a **positive match** can see, whereas widening a **suppression** window widens the laundering surface. That distinction is the lesson of the sentence-scope revert and the `VERBATIM_OPEN` bug, applied deliberately.
- **Result: exactly the three sites the assumption-auditor found by hand, zero false positives.**

Blocking is now **103 = 21 declared rendering lag + 2 stale memo claims + 77 spec pointers + 3 retirement sites** — an honest number rather than an alarming one. **What the two checks buy: B1(vi)'s half-done retirement and every stale spec pointer would now be caught the moment they are introduced, rather than two dispatches later by a critic reading by hand.** That closes the class the last three rounds kept reopening.

**Dispatched with the judgement call stated rather than the answer.** The block holds at least three kinds of thing: **mathematical furniture** a referee must be able to read — (P1)–(P9), the transfer rule, the $o_P$ and $O_P$ companions, the freezing lemma with two clauses and two proofs; **working records** valuable to the next author and not to a referee — change logs, the "why the proof does not survive unchanged" diagnosis, the per-site hypothesis audit; and **things that may be neither**, like the consumer lists. **Test supplied:** *would a referee's understanding of a proof step change if this were absent?* And a tie-break: **where unsure, prefer compiling it — the failure this closes was material being invisible, and over-correcting is cheaper than repeating it.**

**Three constraints that matter more than the move itself.** The compiled material needs **numbered homes with labels**, so the existing citations resolve to something a reader can reach — *"if a citation currently points at a convention by name only, give that convention a numbered home."* **The hypothesis shapes must survive intact** — (F-i) with no integrability, (F-ii) with the conditional envelope, the $O_P$ companion's $\mathcal G$-measurable $V_n$, the composite measurability hypothesis — because **two rounds got exactly these wrong and the compiled version is the one a referee will hold it to.** And the checker's MINORs about this block's own prose **now matter more because they become visible**: the quantifier order it called *"formal only, and self-cancelling"*, and the "nothing downstream changes" list. Told to re-read both **as a referee would, not as a maintainer.**

**Forward note, not this round's work:** the Appendix decision also implies the eventual `paper/main.tex` structure, and there is still no `main.tex` in this repo. ### 2026-07-31 — USER DECISIONS: structure follows CFF; the manuscript is Quarto. Skeleton drafted, one clash fixed, one open.
**CFF's structure read from the PDF rather than assumed:** 1 Introduction · 2 Local identification under independence · 3 Estimation · 4 Asymptotic properties · 5 Monte Carlo · 6 Estimation of returns to education · 7 Conclusions · Appendix A · References. **Two findings that bear on copying it.** (a) **CFF's Appendix A is TWO LINES — a DOI pointer — and the main text contains EXACTLY ONE proof.** Statements are in the article; proofs are in the online supplement. Against our **312 compiled pages**, the supplement route is not a preference but what the volume forces. (b) **CFF §6 is an empirical application and we have none** — a positioning decision, not a gap to fill, so it is left unwritten rather than stubbed.

**Quarto shell drafted at `paper/qmd/`** — `_quarto.yml`, `main.qmd`, `supplement.qmd`. **Design decided by measurement, not preference: the theory corpus carries 136 theorem-like environments and 2,038 `\cref`/`\eqref` calls.** Translating that to markdown would destroy the cross-reference graph **and every tool built this session** — `check_rendering_sync.py`'s label-based CHECK 1/4/6/8, the 117 specs' source pointers, and every narrow critic's line citations. **So the `.qmd` files own the prose and `\input` the theory verbatim inside raw LaTeX blocks**, which pandoc passes through untouched. Recorded in the config, with the explicit rule: **do not use Quarto's native theorem syntax** — it maintains its own counter and label scheme and would collide with the 136 amsthm environments. One theorem system.

**Clash 1, diagnosed and FIXED: `unicode-math`.** Quarto's xelatex template loads it unconditionally (`main.tex:16`), and it fights the project preamble's `lmodern` + T1 + `amssymb` + `dsfont` setup, dying on `\g__um_prime_font_cmd_tl`. **That preamble is pdflatex-native and merely *tolerates* xelatex because the scratch driver never loads unicode-math.** Setting `pdf-engine: pdflatex` makes pandoc take the `\ifPDFTeX` branch and the two agree. Reason recorded in `_quarto.yml`, including that if xelatex is ever needed here the fix is a **custom pandoc template, not a preamble change** — the preamble is shared with the working build.

**Clash 2, located and OPEN: `titlesec`.** `main.log:1899`, *"Package titlesec Error: No format for this command"*, fatal. The project preamble loads `titlesec` for the section styling `working-paper-format.md` requires but issues no `\titleformat`; pandoc's template touches a sectioning command that titlesec then cannot format. **Two candidate fixes, and the choice matters:** supply explicit `\titleformat` declarations (keeps the shared preamble intact, adds to it), or exclude `titlesec` from the qmd path only (leaves the LaTeX build untouched but forks the styling). **Not attempted — I would rather hand over a located defect than a blind third attempt.**

**State: `scripts/build-theory/build.sh` still builds the corpus cleanly at 312 pages.** The Quarto path is additive and nothing depends on it yet.

**Both live traps given explicitly, because both are in the sources it will read.**
1. **The necessity overclaim** — the strategy memo says `prp:sharpquad` supplies *"the necessity half"* in **six** places and the theory files deny it in **five**. Gave it the narrower true statement (the direct bound is order-sharp, so A5(ii) cannot be relaxed *by finding a better bound on the remainder*), and told it plainly: **"I propagated this error into my own briefs before a critic caught it. Do not repeat it."**
2. **The feasible-$n$ disagreement** — $\sqrt n\delta_n^2$ increases across every feasible sample size, turning over near $n\approx10^{58}$, so **`cor:ratefloor`, not `thm:rootn`, describes any sample size anyone will run.** Told it this belongs in §4's scope discussion **in the paper's own voice**, because *"a referee who discovers it unaided will read it as concealment."*

**Three substantive requirements, each drawn from what the critics established rather than from the memo.** §2 must carry what A1's **conditional independence** buys that the mean-zero half does not — it is what makes $J=E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']$ and what retired A3 — and that `prp:nophifree` is an equivalence and **not** an impossibility theorem, so the tilting condition is *reported, not asserted*. §3 must **state plainly what is assumed to exist and not constructed** — $\hat\ell$ priced by A5(iv) and constructed nowhere, $\hat\varepsilon_0$ with no supplier — because the theory says so in terms and the paper must too. §4 must carry the strict frontier, the sandwich, trap 2, and the point that **the positivity condition is implied by the hypotheses rather than assumed.**

INV-7 flagged hardest (**the register is at C1–C79 and every symbol is declared — do not coin one**), plus INV-8, INV-11, INV-22. Quarto note included: prose is markdown, the raw blocks and `\input` lines are already in place and are not to be touched, and **Quarto's native theorem syntax is forbidden — 136 `amsthm` environments, one theorem system.**

**Two caveats passed as things to carry into the guide rather than resolve.** Both papers are **co-authored** — CFF has three authors — so what is extracted is a *collaborative* voice, and the guide must **say so rather than claim to have isolated the author's own hand**. And two papers is the skill's minimum, not a comfortable sample, so it must **mark what it can evidence in both separately from what appears in one — a pattern in one paper is a hypothesis.**

**The question that makes this worth doing, stated plainly in the brief:** the drafting so far calibrated to the **theory corpus** — the author's own *drafting* prose, 312 pages — reaching median sentence 24 words and `The`-openers 19%. **Published prose may differ from drafting prose in ways neither of us can see from inside it, and that gap is exactly what this extraction is for.** Asked for the comparison explicitly: if the published papers are tighter, hedge differently, or open paragraphs differently, that is the most useful thing it can report.

Weighted toward what §1 and the abstract will need: **how an introduction opens and states a contribution**; **how a theory paper narrates a result it cannot fully deliver** (CFF has assumed-not-constructed objects too); **whether the published prose hedges**, since the surrounding corpus does not; citation convention; and paragraph opening moves. Standing rule repeated: **ground every pattern in a quoted example, write `[insufficient corpus evidence]` where there is none, and do not import what the writer thinks good style is.**

### 2026-07-31 — style guide extracted from the author's own papers. **`.claude/references/personal-style-guide.md` is no longer a template.**
7,263 words, every section filled from corpus evidence, **no `[insufficient corpus evidence]` needed.**

**Two corrections to my framing of the corpus.** Paper 2 is **solo-authored** (`\author{Samuele Centorrino}`, "All remaining errors are mine") — so there *is* a solo voice sample and the guide is not purely collaborative. But it is **an unfinished draft** with two live `[PUT REFERENCES HERE]` placeholders. **Consequence it drew and I would not have: the published/draft axis is now confounded with the coauthored/solo axis** — CFF is published-and-three-handed, Exogeneity is draft-and-solo, so **every difference has two candidate explanations and N=2 cannot separate them.** It recorded differences as differences and **never attributed them.** It also endorsed excluding the Concina experimental papers: *"an experimental Results section carries obligations no theory section has."*

**On my prior calibration: essentially right, and on the wrong metric.** CFF's introduction is median 22 with 20% `The`-openers against the draft's 24 and 19% — **that calibration holds and needs no change. The median was never the problem.** What differs, verified by me:

- **Em dashes: 31 in the draft against ZERO in ~13,700 words of corpus.** I checked both papers directly — 0 in CFF, 0 in the Exogeneity `.tex`. **The cleanest finding in the extraction and the easiest to fix**; the corpus does the same job three ways (comma-bounded apposition, a short "That is," restart, or parentheses).
- **Paragraph architecture is 2.2× too heavy** — 153 words against 62–71, six sentences against 2.5, and **zero one-sentence paragraphs where the corpus runs 20%.** *"The biggest structural gap, and it is invisible to a median-sentence check. - **The long tail, not the median** — 17% of draft sentences past 40 words against 5–12%.
- Also: `clearly` and "in order to" are **drafting words the published paper strips** (6→1, 8→0); **intensifiers survive publication** ("very", "extremely" in both), which cuts against generic advice — *do not strip them*.

**It refined the writer-critic's rule-of-three ruling rather than overturning it.** The critic declined to deduct because the theory corpus enumerates constantly. **The published papers do not — "N things follow" appears zero times in either** — but they *do* enumerate loosely ("First… Finally…" with no "Second"). So it is **a drafting-versus-published register mismatch, not an error**: thin them, do not strip them, and the looser style is recorded in the guide rather than a prohibition.

**One thing to fix before §1, and I verified it: Florens, Racine & Centorrino (2018), *Nonparametric instrumental variable derivative estimation* — the author's own paper, cited THREE times in CFF and load-bearing there (it supplies the step-size bound and is the benchmark against which CFF declares its rate equivalent to the linear case) — is ABSENT from the project bibliography.** Zero hits on "racine". ### 2026-08-09/10 — Phase 5 complete (theorist ×7, theorist-critic ×6): `proofs.tex` retyped to L categories
**Phase:** Execution (theory, L-category generalization)
**Target:** `proofs.tex` in five groups, plus 5-O1, Phase 4b, 4c, 5f
**Score:** 5a **94** · 5b **84** · 5c **88** · 5d **84** · 5e **82**. All clear 80. **Zero CRITICAL in any group.**
**Verdict:** **38 of 38 printed statements were already correct at general L, and no result was silently changed at any point.** Every group was a retype of stale proofs — §3b N5's "derive, do not adopt" applied **nowhere** in Phase 5. Build clean, **390 → 423 pages**; binary gate 1 holds at 56/56 throughout.
**The recurring defect was never the mathematics.** Three species dominated, and the token census the plan started with could see only the first: (1) tokens/arity; (2) **order** — `ν_{W̃c}` and `ν_{cW̃}` match the same token, so a token census is structurally blind to them; (3) **claims about the artifact the artifact contradicts** — miscounts, stale provenance, "the proof names N" where it names N+1. I built four censuses (order, arity, scalar-op-on-vector, constant); the **constant** census is the highest-yield per line and the only one that would have caught the N2 family. **A fifth class — self-falsifying arithmetic — bit four times, twice inside ranges already cleared, and no string census finds it.** Rule adopted: re-count every enumerative claim against the artifact in the same pass that touches either.
**Load-bearing finds:** `Υ` applied to an object carrying two indices when it takes one — **non-conformable at every L including 2**, surviving because a token census matches `Υ` regardless of what follows. `eq:convmean`'s constant short by `‖π‖_∞` — a **false** inequality, not a loose one. `eq:biasb1`'s scalar modulus on an ℝᵖ vector, which `thm:rootn` Step 4's triangle inequality **consumes** — a gap at one link of the headline theorem's bias chain, closed by Phase 5f. `lem:adjoint` and `lem:quantu` each had a general-L **statement** with a binary **proof** — proofs that did not prove their statements.
**Nine of my dispatches were overridden by workers. Every override was correct.** The common cause: I scoped from an inherited summary rather than from the artifact. Rules adopted — a dispatch may state a conclusion but never forbid checking the derivation behind it; a discharge may only be asserted by the edit that performs it; a quotation must move in the same pass as its source.
**Stranded items reached nine**, driven by phases closing on files that still had live debts pointing into them. Created Phase 4b (`assumptions_supp.tex`), 4c (closed-file printed debts), 5f (p-arity), and re-scoped Phase C to printed text + `notation_glossary.md` — **a read-only critic can never be an acquirer.**
**Report:** plan §3e

### 2026-08-10 — Phase 6 complete (4 narrow critics + 4 repair passes): theory audited and repaired
**Phase:** Execution (theory) · **Score:** notation **46** · assumptions **30** · regularity **41** · counterexamples **80**
**BINARY GATE 1: CLEARS** (56/56). **COUNTEREXAMPLE GATE: NOT BLOCKED — no confirmed counterexample.** Build clean, **425 → 435 pages**.
**One CRITICAL, and it was real.** (P-g) was a factor `h_u` too weak for the step consuming it: `lem:unifop` Step 4 charges the displacement in operator norm at `c₂c_Π h_u^{−1}·|Δ_{h_x}φ−Δφ|`, so `thm:rootn` inherited a hypothesis not covering its own admissible window. **Two things made it decisive rather than arguable**: the corpus already printed the correct arithmetic forty pages away, and the *sibling* hypothesis (P-c) already carried the factor with a comment reading "the factor `h_u` is not decoration". Repaired across 11 sites in 5 files; threshold moved `s > 31/30 → 11/10`; **both running configurations clear at `s=3` with margins +2.13 and +2.06, so no conclusion is lost.**
**Printed text had been LOST from the shipped supplement.** A prior pass spliced a sentence onto the end of a `%` comment line; it never compiled, and a paragraph in the PDF began with a sentence that had no subject. Found and recovered. This is the `%`-swallowing hazard carried as a *risk* in every dispatch — it had already happened and shipped.
**Other substantive finds:** `ξ_i` Bochner-integrated with strong measurability never established (corpus sweep returned "Pettis" **nowhere**), though `E T̄` is the object every subsequent Part-H display is written at — closed via Pettis from separability already proved at `prp:compact` Step 4. `eq:crossshift`'s printed witness **infeasible under C1(ii)**, a stated hypothesis of its own lemma — and infeasible on the probability alone (`P(Δh=−1)=½` needs `|A||B| ≥ ½`, max is `¼`) before the sign argument. `prp:tccrepair`(iii) and `lem:epterm` both stating results not implied by their listed hypotheses.
**Two things §6 needs that were printed nowhere:** (a) **consistency of every cell set is NECESSARY, not sufficient, for A1** — a DGP exists where A1 fails, every conflict set is empty, and the estimator converges to the wrong φ; the false-negative rate is unpriced. (b) A4(ii)'s upper bound read with A1b(iv) forces `ΔZ` to carry **exactly one non-zero constant coordinate** — the differenced equation must have a period effect.
**A4(i)'s empirical burden, settled:** of eleven consumers, three need only **positivity** and eight need the **value**, and **none of the eight is on the θ-step's inferential path.** So §6 owes positivity at every cell — which `min_l ϱ̂_l = 0.0115` clears — and reporting `ε_ϱ`, `ε_Ω` at realised rather than nominal values.
**Thirteen worker overrides across Phases 4–6; every one correct.** Two were of *my* printed numbers (a `½` carried from a superseded witness, and a Monte Carlo estimate where the exact value is `1/4`) — the same self-falsifying-arithmetic class the dispatch was warning about. One caught an error in an audit's own recommendation: cutting a bound on string-census evidence, when a proof cited it **by description**.
**Report:** `quality_reports/{notation,assumptions,regularity,counterexamples}/`

**Phase:** Execution · **Target:** `sim_multinomial_n1defect.R`, §5.4 of `main.qmd` · **Score:** pending re-score
**Verdict:** **N1 FAILS on all three designs at all three cardinalities** — defect 0.53–0.72 of ‖L⋆‖ against Block I's 3.3e−6 at 𝖫=2 — and the spread *across* cardinality is at most 0.088 on that level, so there is no cardinality-varying mechanism. §5.4's "the cardinality does very little" is therefore **explained by N1 failing everywhere by about the same amount**, not by the theorem being confirmed at 𝖫≥3. **Not a defect in `thm:rootn`:** `solve_balance_weight` builds its RKHS over the residual alone and `crossfit_once` anchors with a **constant**; `rem:anchor`'s z-dependent anchor would restore N1 with the same ℓ and A2′(iii) admits it. Same species as the C3(ii) boundary-kernel gap. New: `se_ratio` table (the entire well-specified coverage shortfall is the interval's **scale**, not the limit law — within ±0.070 at all 54 cells) and Panel B standard errors, which show the "rises at two" claim is **1.50 SE, not resolved from flat**. **Found and closed an armed data-loss hazard:** a comment-only edit to `lf_panel.R` moved the whole-file checkpoint md5, so `SIM_SUMMARISE_ONLY=1` discarded the cache, summarised zero of nine cells and overwrote the finished summary with an empty one. Guard added and tested against the real destructive path; the fingerprint was **not** loosened, because the author's documented reasoning for it is sound and the failure is elsewhere. Executable text of the run-time engine verified byte-identical to live, so the six merged tables stand. **Writer corrected the orchestrator on five points**, two substantive (the spread range, and an N1 certificate relayed as 1e−4 when the printed figure is 1.67e−4). Main **170pp**, 28 captions 1..28, no gaps, zero undefined refs. **Third clipping mechanism identified:** an ordinary unrotated float sheds printed text past **72.27pt** of inked overrun while every check reports CLEAN.
**Report:** tasks `a26c67cf…`, `ad301a40…`

**Verdict:** **Gate-5 verdict re-issued as "Matches at large n only" on 2026-08-27, i.e. WITH the N1 failure in evidence — this is not the stale 2026-08-26 input.** Judged: no cell contradicts the theorem on a design satisfying its hypotheses; the well-specified coverage shortfall is accounted for to rms 0.024 by the standard-error ratio alone; N1's failure is an implementation gap, not a defect in `thm:rootn`. **Strengthened the N1 result:** it does **not** rest on the three certificates but on an analytic argument — Λ has no z argument, so under M3 the Gaussian convolution forces Λ = c a.e. and the realised weight would have L_max = 0 against a measured 2.494–10.593. **The defect is nonzero by theorem, not by measurement.** **Found the certificates over-described:** `GH_Z_LO` is declared a stability comparison and is **dead code**; "two routes that share no line of arithmetic" is **false** (both use the same tensor GH z-rule); the M3-off control is a **degenerate-case floor**, not the computation's numerical floor.
